# PROJECT_SPEC.md — Myanmar Retail POS

> **Status:** Living document. Every implementation change MUST be reflected here in the same change-set.
> **Last updated:** 2026-07-10
> **Owner workstreams:** Frontend/UX · Backend/Security · QA/Spec

---

## 1. Product Overview

An **offline-first Point-of-Sale (POS) application for Myanmar retailers** (grocery, minimarts, pharmacies, phone/accessory shops, teashops). It runs on Android and iOS phones/tablets, works fully without internet, and syncs to the cloud when a connection is available.

### 1.1 Core value

- **Works with no internet.** All core operations (sell, add stock, print receipt) function offline. Sync is opportunistic.
- **Cheap hardware friendly.** Targets low-end Android devices and thermal mini-printers (58mm/80mm ESC/POS) common in Myanmar — Bluetooth, Wi-Fi, or USB on a shop PC.
- **Myanmar-first UX.** Full Burmese (my) + English (en) localization, Myanmar Kyat (MMK) currency, local date formats, and payment methods (KBZPay, WavePay, AYAPay, CBPay, cash).
- **Subscription licensed.** Online license activation with offline grace period; supports local payment collection.

### 1.2 Non-goals (v1)

- No multi-branch consolidated accounting (single shop per license in v1; multi-device same-shop sync is in scope).
- No full ERP / payroll / supplier procurement **automation** (auto-reorder rules, EDI/supplier integration, accounts-payable tracking). A lightweight supplier directory + purchase-order tracking (what was ordered from whom, at what cost, received all-or-nothing into stock) shipped 2026-08-03 — see §12 changelog — and is explicitly in scope; it's manual tracking, not automation.
- No web storefront / e-commerce.

---

## 2. Target Users & Devices

| Persona | Needs |
|---|---|
| Shop owner | Daily/period sales analytics, stock value, license & staff management |
| Cashier | Fast sell screen, barcode/qty entry, receipt printing, cash/mobile payment |
| Stock keeper | Add/adjust inventory, low-stock alerts, purchase entry |

- **OS:** Android 8+ (primary), iOS 14+ (secondary).
- **Form factor:** Phone (portrait) and tablet (landscape). Responsive layout required.
- **Printer:** ESC/POS thermal, 58mm & 80mm paper. **Mobile:** Bluetooth + Wi-Fi (TCP 9100). **Windows PC:** same Wi-Fi printer + USB cable.

---

## 3. Tech Stack

| Layer | Choice | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| App framework | **Flutter 3.44 / Dart 3.12** | Single codebase, Android + iOS |
| State mgmt | **Riverpod** | Testable, compile-safe DI |
| Routing | **go_router** | Declarative, deep-link ready |
| Local DB | **Drift (SQLite)** | Typed, reactive, migration support — source of truth on device |
| Backend | **Supabase** | Postgres + Row Level Security + Auth + Edge Functions |
| Sync | Custom pull/push over Supabase REST + a local outbox queue | Last-write-wins + updated_at, tombstones for deletes |
| Printing | `esc_pos_utils_plus` + `print_bluetooth_thermal` + raw TCP 9100 + Windows RAW USB | Same ESC/POS bytes; transport is Bluetooth (mobile), Wi-Fi, or USB cable (Windows) |
| i18n | Flutter `gen_l10n` (ARB files) | `en`, `my` |
| Analytics/charts | `fl_chart` | Sales dashboards |
| Auth/License | Supabase Auth + `licenses` table + Edge Function `activate` | Device-bound key, JWT claims |

---

## 4. Architecture

### 4.1 Offline-first principle

```
                 ┌─────────────────────────── Device (source of truth) ──────────────────────────┐
   UI (Flutter)  →  Riverpod controllers  →  Repositories  →  Drift (SQLite)  →  Outbox table
                                                                     │                  │
                                                                     │ (read models)    │ (queued mutations)
                                                                     ▼                  ▼
                                                              Reactive streams     SyncEngine
                                                                                        │  (when online + licensed)
                                                                                        ▼
                                                                                   Supabase (Postgres/RLS)
```

- The **UI never talks to the network directly.** It reads/writes Drift. Repositories enqueue changes to an **outbox**; the `SyncEngine` drains the outbox and pulls remote changes on a schedule / connectivity change.
- **Conflict policy (v1):** last-write-wins by `updated_at` (server clock authoritative on push ack). Money-critical rows (sales) are **append-only** and never updated after finalization to avoid conflicts; corrections are separate reversal records.

### 4.2 Layered structure

```
lib/
  core/            # theme, constants, errors, utils, result types
  l10n/            # generated localizations
  data/
    local/         # Drift database, DAOs, tables
    remote/        # Supabase client, DTOs, mappers
    sync/          # SyncEngine, outbox, connectivity
    repositories/  # one repo per domain, offline-first
  domain/          # entities, value objects (Money, Barcode), use-cases
  features/
    auth/          # license activation + staff PIN login
    inventory/     # products, categories, stock
    sales/         # sell screen, cart, checkout
    invoices/      # receipt model + printing
    analytics/     # dashboards
    settings/      # shop profile, printer, language, license status
  app.dart         # router, theme, providers root
  main.dart
```

### 4.3 Money & rounding

- Store money as **integer minor units? No** — MMK has no widely used minor unit; store as integer **kyat** (`int`). A `Money` value object wraps `int amountKyat`. All arithmetic in integers to avoid float drift. Display with thousands separators.

### 4.4 Per-shop SQLite + device sidecar (locked)

**Decision (2026-08-06):** cut over from a single `mm_pos.sqlite` to:

| File | Holds |
|---|---|
| `mm_pos_device.sqlite` | Device-global settings only: printer/label, `device.id`, `license.json`, `app.locale`, onboarding, `branch.switch.state`, vendor config cache, etc. (`SettingsRepository.isDeviceGlobalKey`) |
| `mm_pos_<shopId>.sqlite` | Synced business tables + shop-scoped settings (receipt header, track_stock, staff role/PIN keys, sync cursors, …) |

**Branch / account switch:** close current shop DB → open target shop file (create empty if missing) → Riverpod rebinds via `DatabaseSession` → incremental sync. **Do not wipe** other shops' files.

**Account wipe-and-claim (sign into a different shop's account):** same as switch — reopen target shop file; leave other shop files on disk. Outbox precheck still runs on the *from* shop before close.

**Legacy migrate (one-shot on open):** if `mm_pos.sqlite` exists and sidecar/shop files are missing, copy device keys → sidecar, copy file → `mm_pos_<currentShopId>.sqlite` (strip device keys from the shop copy), rename legacy to `mm_pos.sqlite.bak`.

**Deferred:** making `switch_token` required on `activate` (optional until old clients are gone).

---

## 5. Data Model (v1)

All tables carry: `id` (uuid), `shop_id` (uuid), `created_at`, `updated_at`, `is_deleted` (bool, tombstone), `dirty` (local-only sync flag).

- **shops** — id, name, address, phone, currency='MMK', logo, receipt_footer.
- **staff** — id, shop_id, name, pin_hash, role (owner/cashier/stock), active.
- **categories** — id, shop_id, name, sort.
- **products** — id, shop_id, name, sku, barcode, category_id, cost_price, sale_price, wholesale_price?, vip_price?, unit, image, is_active. Tier prices are nullable overrides — null falls back to `sale_price`.
- **stock_levels** — product_id, quantity (int), reorder_level. (Denormalized current qty; movements are the ledger.)
- **stock_movements** — id, product_id, type (purchase/sale/adjustment/return), qty_delta, unit_cost, ref_id, note. `unit_cost` is a real FIFO-weighted average as of the FIFO cost basis feature (was always 0 before).
- **stock_lots** — *local-only, not in Supabase.* seq, product_id, remaining_qty, unit_cost. The device's own open FIFO purchase lots, oldest-first — fully derivable by replaying `stock_movements`, so never synced (syncing an absolute per-lot quantity via LWW would repeat `stock_levels`' own known anti-pattern).
- **sales** — id, shop_id, invoice_no, staff_id, subtotal, discount, total, paid, change, payment_method, customer_name?, note, finalized_at. **Append-only.**
- **sale_items** — id, sale_id, product_id, name_snapshot, price_snapshot, qty, line_total, cost_snapshot?. `cost_snapshot` is the line's total FIFO cost of goods sold (null on sales predating the feature — Analytics falls back to the product's flat `cost_price`).
- **payments** — id, sale_id, method (cash/kbzpay/wavepay/ayapay/cbpay), amount, ref_no.
- **licenses** — id, shop_id, key, plan (monthly/yearly), status (active/expired/grace), activated_at, expires_at, device_id, last_verified_at.

**Supabase RLS:** every table scoped by `shop_id = auth.jwt() ->> 'shop_id'`. No cross-shop reads.

---

## 6. Licensing & Subscription

- **Model:** one shop, bound to devices at activation. The **main phone is included and does not count**; **2 extra phones or computers** (owner or staff login) are free — **3 devices total**. A phone and a computer each count as one extra. Further devices need a one-time fee (`device.extra_fee`). Plans: monthly / yearly.
- **Activation flow:** enter key → Edge Function `activate(key, device_id)` validates + binds → returns signed license payload → stored locally.
- **Offline grace:** app works offline; license re-verified when online. After `expires_at` plus a **7-day grace**, the shop auto-downgrades to the **Free plan** — Sell and Inventory keep working; Premium stays locked until renewed. There is no till-locking read-only mode.
- **Local payment support:** owner can record a renewal payment (KBZPay/Wave/cash) which flags the license for manual/automated reconciliation server-side. (Automated gateway integration is a later phase.)
- **Anti-abuse:** device binding, server-side expiry authoritative, key never trusted from client alone.

### 6.1 Referral commission (Phase 6)

- **Model:** single-level. Every license carries a shareable `referral_code` (`REF-XXXX`). A new shop may enter a referrer's code in its subscription request (`license_requests.referred_by_code`).
- **Accrual (payment-tied only):** each time a referred shop's payment is fulfilled (admin `fulfill_request`), the referrer earns a commission = `paid_amount × referral.rate` (default 15%, in `app_config`). One immutable row per paid request in `referral_commissions`, deduped by a unique `source_request_id`. **No commission is ever created for recruitment alone** — this keeps it a legitimate affiliate program, not a pyramid scheme.
- **Payout = license credit:** the referrer redeems the accumulated balance into whole months on their own license via `redeem_referral_balance()` (self-service, whole `price.monthly` units; remainder stays as balance). Draws recorded in `referral_redemptions`; balance = earned − redeemed.
- **RLS:** a shop reads only its own `referrals` / commissions / redemptions (`referrer_shop_id = auth_shop_id()`); balance readout via SECURITY DEFINER `my_referral_balance()`. All writes are service-role (admin Edge Function) or the definer RPCs.
- **App surface:** Settings → *Refer & earn* — running earnings wallet, progress toward the next free month, one-tap redeem, code share.
- **Commission alert:** a local notification (`flutter_local_notifications`, no FCM) fires when the server-side earned total grows — polled at launch, on resume, and every 30 min, deduped by a `referral.seen_earned` watermark. Delivered next time the app opens; true background push is a later phase.
- **Admin tooling:** a **Referrals** tab in the admin dashboard ([`lib/admin/`](lib/admin/)) — commissions grouped by referrer (lifetime earned + payment count) with one-click **Apply credit** (`apply_referral_credit`), plus the raw referral links. Commission rate/toggle editable under **Config** (`referral.rate`, `referral.enabled`).

---

## 7. Receipt Printing

- ESC/POS command generation via `esc_pos_utils_plus`. Same byte stream, three transports:
  - **Bluetooth** — `print_bluetooth_thermal`, iOS/Android. Pair in system settings, pick from Printer settings.
  - **Wi-Fi** — raw TCP to the printer's LAN IP, port **9100** (JetDirect). Opening Printer settings on the Wi-Fi tab scans the current subnet so same-network printers appear in the list; IP can still be typed if a printer does not answer. Phone and computer can share one Wi-Fi printer by entering the same IP. iOS prompts via `NSLocalNetworkUsageDescription`.
  - **USB cable** — Windows shop PC only. Lists printers Windows already knows and sends RAW ESC/POS (`windows_printer`, `useRawDatatype: true`) so the driver does not re-paginate a receipt.
- Support 58mm (32 char) and 80mm (48 char) profiles — configurable in settings, remembered per printer.
- Dedicated TSPL label printers use the same Bluetooth / Wi-Fi / USB transports as the receipt printer (TSPL bytes instead of ESC/POS).
- Receipt includes: shop name/logo, address/phone, invoice no, datetime, cashier, line items (name/qty/price/total), subtotal/discount/total, payment method + change, footer text, optional QR.
- **Burmese text on thermal printers** is a known hard problem (many printers lack Myanmar font). Strategy: render the receipt (or the Burmese portions) to a **bitmap image** and print as raster, so any Unicode renders correctly regardless of printer font support. English/numeric parts may use native text mode for speed.

---

## 8. Internationalization

- Languages: **en**, **my** (Burmese, Unicode — NOT Zawgyi). ARB files under `lib/l10n/`.
- All user-facing strings via generated `AppLocalizations`. No hardcoded strings in widgets.
- Numbers/currency: MMK formatting, optional Myanmar digit display toggle.
- QA workstream owns translation completeness + accuracy verification (see §11).

---

## 9. Security

- Supabase Auth (anonymous/device session bootstrapped by license activation).
- **RLS on every table.** Server never trusts client-supplied `shop_id`; derived from JWT.
- Staff PIN stored as salted hash (e.g. `bcrypt`/`argon2` via crypto lib) — never plaintext.
- Secrets (Supabase anon key is public by design; service key NEVER in app). Edge Functions hold privileged logic.
- Input validation on Edge Functions. Rate-limit `activate`.
- Local DB: consider SQLCipher for at-rest encryption (phase 3).

---

## 10. Delivery Phases

> Build order. Each phase ends in a runnable, demoable state. Spec updated per phase.

- **Phase 0 — Foundation (scaffold):** Flutter project, folders, theme, Riverpod, go_router, l10n (en/my), Drift DB skeleton, Supabase client config, CI-friendly analysis options. *Runnable empty-shell app.*
- **Phase 1 — Inventory:** categories + products CRUD, stock levels, low-stock alerts, barcode field, offline persistence. Seed/demo data.
- **Phase 2 — Sales / Sell screen:** cart, qty/discount, checkout, payment method, finalize sale (append-only), stock decrement, invoice number generation. Offline.
- **Phase 3 — Invoices & Bluetooth printing:** receipt model, ESC/POS generation, printer pairing/settings, Burmese raster fallback, reprint.
- **Phase 4 — Sync engine:** Supabase schema + RLS migrations, outbox drain, pull/merge, connectivity handling, conflict rules.
- **Phase 5 — Licensing:** licenses table, `activate` Edge Function, activation UI, grace/read-only enforcement, local renewal payment record.
- **Phase 6 — Analytics:** sales dashboards (daily/period), top products, profit (sale−cost), stock value, fl_chart visualizations.
- **Phase 7 — Hardening:** tests, security review, i18n QA pass, at-rest encryption, performance on low-end devices, release builds.

## 11. Workstreams (the three sub-agent roles)

### 11.1 Frontend / UX
- Responsive layouts (phone portrait + tablet landscape), Material 3 theme, Myanmar-appropriate typography (Pyidaungsu/Noto Sans Myanmar).
- **Tablet (medium ≥ 640):** NavigationRail; Sell grid|cart split; Orders/Invoices master–detail; Inventory denser grid; `showAppModal` / `ContentWidth` chrome. Smoke: `docs/tablet_smoke.md` (#79).
- **Desktop:** Windows-only path (CI build + later PC smoke). macOS deferred. See `docs/windows/` (#80).
- Fast, low-friction sell screen; big tap targets; works one-handed.
- Consistency: shared widgets, spacing scale, theme tokens.

### 11.2 Backend / Security
- Supabase schema, RLS policies, Edge Functions (`activate`, sync helpers).
- Sync engine correctness, conflict handling, outbox reliability.
- License auth, device binding, vulnerability patching, secret hygiene.

### 11.3 QA / Spec
- Keep THIS file in sync with code every change.
- Translation accuracy (en/my) — no missing keys, correct Burmese.
- Functional + unit/widget tests; bug triage; release checklist.

## 13. Security Review (Phase 7)

Manual review of the current codebase. Status: no high-severity issues in app code.

**Good:**
- **No service-role key in the app.** Only the anon/publishable key ships (public
  by design). Privileged logic (device binding, `shop_id` claim) lives in the
  `activate` Edge Function, which alone uses `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`.
- **RLS on every synced table**, scoped by `auth_shop_id()` from the JWT. The
  server never trusts a client-supplied `shop_id`.
- **License authority is server-side** — expiry/binding validated in the Edge
  Function; the client cache is advisory only.
- **Money is integer kyat** — no floating-point rounding on financial values.
- Anon key kept out of source (`env.local.json` gitignored).

**Action items before production (tracked):**
1. **At-rest DB encryption — blocked on Flutter/sqlite3 tooling, not app code.**
   Investigated 2026-07-28: the plan this doc previously described
   (`sqlite3_flutter_libs` → `sqlcipher_flutter_libs`) is no longer viable —
   `sqlcipher_flutter_libs` is now published as EOL (`0.7.0+eol`, pub.dev
   description: *"Not used anymore, update to version 3.x of package:sqlite3
   instead"*). The maintainer-recommended replacement requires:
   - Bumping `sqlite3` to 3.x and opting into Dart's **native-assets**
     build system via a new `hooks:` section in `pubspec.yaml`
     (`hooks.user_defines.sqlite3.source: sqlcipher`).
   - Enabling `flutter config --enable-native-assets` — a **machine-wide**
     toggle (not scoped to this project), and still an opt-in/experimental
     feature on the stable channel as of Flutter 3.44.8, not default-on.
   - Runtime key-setting (`PRAGMA key` equivalent) isn't clearly documented
     for the new hook-based build — would need to be worked out empirically.
   - SQLCipher links OpenSSL on Android/Windows/Linux — extra build surface
     and its own license, on top of SQLCipher's own.
   Given the parallel to the still-open macOS desktop rendering issue (also
   bleeding-edge-tooling-shaped), deliberately **not** pursuing this now —
   revisit once native-assets/hooks are default-on and the SQLCipher runtime
   API is documented, or reconsider a lower-risk fallback (application-level
   field encryption for the most sensitive columns only, using the
   `cryptography` package already in `pubspec.yaml`, instead of whole-file
   encryption) if this becomes a hard production requirement sooner.

Resolved (corrected here — this list had gone stale; each of these was
actually already done in an earlier phase but never checked off):
- ~~Remove dev-open policies~~ — done 2026-07-13, migration
  `0012_drop_dev_policies.sql` (see that day's changelog entry).
- ~~Wire crash reporting~~ — done 2026-07-13, `lib/main.dart`'s
  `FlutterError.onError` forwards to `Sentry.captureException` (DSN-gated).
- ~~Rate-limit `activate`~~ — done 2026-07-28: 10 attempts per IP per 15
  minutes, `activate_attempts` table (`0037`), same pattern as the
  storefront's own rate limit.
- ~~Staff PIN hashing~~ — done 2026-07-28: `StaffMembers.pin` now stores
  `sha256(memberId:pin)` (salted by the member's own id, a random UUID — no
  separate salt column needed), not plaintext. There never was a separate
  `pin_hash` column as this doc previously claimed — hashing happens in place
  on the existing `pin` column. A row written before this change (plaintext,
  no `v1:` prefix) verifies once against the plaintext and is upgraded to a
  hash automatically — no separate migration script needed.

**Onboarding first-frame layout crash — found via tablet testing, fixed (2026-07-28):**
investigating tablet (iPad) layout on Xcode's iOS Simulator (no physical iPad
available) surfaced a real bug: the onboarding screen's bottom bar (page dots +
`Spacer()` + `FilledButton`) threw `BoxConstraints forces an infinite width`
on the app's very first frame, leaving the whole screen blank except "Skip"
and one dot. Initially suspected this was a tablet-width app bug, then
(wrongly) concluded it was a machine-local Flutter engine quirk unrelated to
the app, after it reproduced identically on a phone-width iPhone Simulator
too and survived a device rotation. That was corrected after also
reproducing it on a **freshly created Android tablet emulator (Pixel Tablet,
API 34)** — a completely different rendering engine (Skia/Impeller-GLES vs.
iOS's Metal) hitting the exact same symptom rules out "iOS/macOS engine bug"
and confirms a real, if narrow, app-level layout issue. Traced the actual
stack (not just the top-line assertion): the crash originates inside
`FilledButton`'s own internal tap-target sizing (`_RenderInputPadding` in
Flutter's `button_style_button.dart`), which — on this app's very first
build, before the engine has necessarily settled on final metrics — computed
an infinite intrinsic width instead of falling back to its content width.
Once thrown, the exception aborts that frame's layout, and because nothing
in this static screen ever triggers a further rebuild, it stays blank
forever (matches why it reproduces 100% of the time in emulators but never
on a real device, where photo evidence of an actual production install shows
this race apparently never wins). Fixed by wrapping the `FilledButton` in
`ConstrainedBox(constraints: BoxConstraints(maxWidth: 220))` — this hands
the button's internals a concrete, finite width no matter what its own
sizing computes, with no visible change in the normal case since the
button's natural width is far below 220. Verified fixed on both a fresh
iPad Air 11" (M4) Simulator and Android Pixel Tablet emulator (`adb
screencap`) — full onboarding content (icon, title, body, all page dots,
Skip, Next/Get Started) now renders correctly on both. Two dead-end fixes
were tried and reverted first: a `SizedBox`-pinned width (still inherited
whatever the ambient issue was, via `BoxConstraints.enforce`'s clamp
semantics) and an `OverflowBox` (fixed the width but introduced a *new*
self-inflicted crash by leaving height unbounded — `OverflowBox` needs both
axes specified or it inherits Column's normal "unbounded main-axis height for
non-flex children" behavior and tries to size itself to infinity). The
targeted `ConstrainedBox`-on-the-button fix avoids both traps. No widget
test added: the trigger is a real-environment first-frame timing race, not
something a synthetic `flutter test` harness reproduces deterministically —
verified instead via the same manual-emulator method that found it, per this
project's existing convention for UI-only changes.

| 2026-09-04 | 315 | **Closed the last of the idempotency theme: the storefront order submit and the `/renew` licence request now carry a browser-generated id, so a retry resolves to the request that already exists instead of making a second one.** These were the two remaining instances of the shape #314 documented — a client bound at 15s (`kEdgeInvokeTimeout`) in front of a handler doing 6+ sequential round trips plus a possible cold start, so an order that commits at ~16s reads to the customer as a plain failure. The button re-enables, the message is generic, they tap Place Order again, and the shop packs and ships **two orders for one transfer**. On `/renew` the stake is higher: two pending renewals for one payment, and an admin confirming both mints two licences (`create_license` has no shop_id uniqueness guard). Both handlers now accept a client id (`client_order_id` / `client_request_id`), look for an existing row first, and replay the original response — `order_no` and line items for an order, `request_id`/`invoice_no` for a renewal — rather than inserting again; a caller that sends nothing keeps the old behaviour. The pages generate the id once per checkout and reuse it across retries (`_clientOrderId ??=`), which is the whole point: a fresh id per attempt would defeat it. Same convention the sync outbox has always used ("every row has a client-generated UUID (idempotent retries)", CLAUDE.md). Verifying the replay path caught a wrong column name in my own first draft — the order-items table stores `price_snapshot`, not `unit_price`, so the replayed lines would have come back with null prices. **Honest gap**: `edge_money_idempotency_test.dart` only scans money-moving *RPC* calls, and these two are direct table inserts, so the new guards are not covered by that test — the guard's own blind spot, recorded rather than papered over. `flutter analyze` clean, 781 tests pass, `deno check` clean on the storefront function. |
| 2026-09-04 | 314 | **Audited the four surfaces that had never been reviewed — admin console, customer storefront, invoices_web, and the least-tested mobile areas — and fixed the worst of what came back. ~42 findings; the owner asked to work through all four groups.** Answering "is anything still unexamined?" honestly required measuring rather than guessing: the UI/UX sweep (#300-309) had covered only the mobile app's six tabs, leaving `lib/admin/` (4,299 LOC, one test file), `lib/storefront/` (3,118, ~none) and `lib/invoices_web/` (929, ~none) untouched — all three money- or customer-facing. **The theme, and my own contribution to it.** The 15s `invokeBounded` timeout I added in #310 did not create these bugs, but it made a latent class *reachable*: a timeout tells the user it failed, so they retry, and nothing on the other side is idempotent. Three instances. `extend_license`'s dedup window was **10s — shorter than my own 15s client timeout**, so an extend that committed at t=8s timed out at t=15s and was re-clicked at t≈20s with the window already shut: 12 paid months, 24 granted. Widened to 90s (must exceed the client timeout *and* the human "did that work?" interval) and documented against `kEdgeInvokeTimeout` so the two can't drift apart again. I should have checked that interaction when I set the timeout. **The CRITICAL was worse and not mine**: `fulfill_request` returns HTTP 200 with `request_marked_fulfilled: false` when the licence minted but the request row could not be closed — the server comment says the caller "must be told this didn't fully succeed" — and the client read only `data['key']`, so the request reappeared as pending, the admin clicked Confirm again, and `create_license` (no shop_id uniqueness guard) minted a **second licence for one payment**. Now throws `RequestNotClosedException`, surfaced as a dialog carrying the already-issued key plus an explicit "do NOT confirm again". Also: a suppressed duplicate extend was reported as a plain success (`duplicate: true` was dropped), so a shop buying two separate 1-month top-ups in quick succession was shown "Extended" twice and given one month. **Money rendered wrongly.** `invoice_detail_web_screen` read the currency from `storefronts` — a table that defaults to MMK and only exists once a shop publishes a storefront (Premium) — instead of the authoritative `shop_profiles`. For a THB shop with no storefront, 12550 minor units printed as **"12,550 Ks" instead of "฿125.50"**, on screen and on the A4 PDF handed to the customer, while the sibling list screen showed `฿`. Separately, the referral screen formatted commission balances with the shop's **POS** currency: commissions and licence prices are Ks server-side (migration 0013) because the vendor bills in kyat regardless of what a shop sells in, so a 30,000 Ks balance read as "300.00 ฿". Both now use the right source. **Blocked flows.** `compressImage` returns the original bytes and extension when it cannot decode — which is exactly HEIC, the **default iPhone photo format** that `FileType.image` offers on iOS Safari. The upload went out as `.heic`, the bucket (jpeg/png/webp only, migration 0032) refused it, and the customer got a generic error that no retry could clear, with no way to detach the proof; on `/renew` the owner had already transferred the money. Both pickers now check format and size *before* upload and say what to do instead. `resolveOrCreate` used `getSingleOrNull`, which **throws** on duplicate customer names — and nothing prevents duplicates (no uniqueness check; two offline devices both sync in a "Ma Aye") — so every credit sale to a duplicated name failed permanently behind a generic error; it now resolves to the oldest match. A purchase-order line saved with qty 0 made `receivePO` roll back forever (`adjustStock` throws on a zero delta); the editor now clamps to ≥1. **Data loss.** Backup restore is a replace-all with no shop check, and backup filenames are bare timestamps — restoring shop A's file into shop B deleted B's rows, inserted A's (invisible to every `shop_id`-scoped read), and left the outbox pushing A's rows against B's JWT claim forever, all while reporting success. Exports now record `shopId` and restore refuses a foreign file (`ShopMismatchException`); legacy files without the field still restore, since they make no claim to check. Restore also had no equivalent of branch-switching's `assertSafeToClear`, so an offline device's un-pushed sales were deleted and their outbox entries then dropped as "local row gone" — it now refuses while writes are pending (`UnsyncedDataException`). Both refusals get their own message rather than the generic failure. 7 new regression tests. `flutter analyze` clean, 781 tests pass (774 + 7). **Still open, deliberately**: the storefront order submit and `/renew` licence request have no idempotency key at all (the same retry-duplicates shape, fix needs a client-generated id plus an Edge Function short-circuit), and the remaining ~25 lower-severity findings across the four audits. |

| 2026-09-04 | 311 | **#310's CI gate paid for itself on its very first run: it failed, and the failure was six real escaped-error bugs in the printer.** CI's `channel: stable` resolved to Flutter 3.47.2 against 3.44.8 locally, and the newer analyzer ships `unawaited_return_in_try_block` — which fired six times in `printer_service.dart`, all the same shape: `return _send(bytes, …);` (and `return decodeLogoImage(…)`) **inside** a `try` whose `catch` exists precisely to turn a failure into a friendly result. Returning the future unawaited means the function has already returned by the time it completes, so the `catch` never sees the error: it escapes as an unhandled exception instead. `_send` is the part that actually talks to the Bluetooth/network printer — i.e. the single most likely thing to fail (printer off, out of paper, out of range) — so **every print path (receipt, sales report, Z-report, and both label formats) reported hardware failures as an uncaught exception rather than the `PrintResult(false, message)` the code was written to produce**; `_fetchLogo` likewise contradicted its own doc comment's promise that "a bad image just means the receipt prints without a logo, not that printing fails." Fixed with `return await` at all six sites. **Also pinned CI to Flutter 3.47.2**: a gate whose toolchain floats can start failing on a day nobody changed anything, which is the one thing a gate must never do — bumps are now a deliberate, reviewable commit that lands the new lints on purpose. Note the local SDK (3.44.8) is now older than CI's, so `flutter analyze` locally is weaker than the gate until it's upgraded to match. `flutter analyze` clean, all 767 tests pass. |

real but narrow-window and would need server-side (RLS or trigger) work to close, noted for a future pass rather than attempted here. Caught immediately by checking the deployed page title rather than trusting a green `vercel deploy` exit code; fixed by explicitly `vercel link --project allinonepos-shop` before redeploying `shop.allinonepos.app` correctly, then rebuilding the `invoices` target fresh and redeploying it back to `allinonepos-invoices` to undo the wrong content. Both live-verified afterward via the Browser tool: `shop.allinonepos.app` title "Shop", `invoices.allinonepos.app` title "Invoices", zero console errors on either. **Lesson for next time**: always run `vercel link --project <name>` immediately before `vercel deploy` when reusing `build/web` across different targets in one session — never assume the existing `.vercel` link still matches the content just built into that directory. |

| 2026-08-30 | 275 | **Verified the storefront function's X-Forwarded-For trust assumption live, and hardened the new Call button.** #274's contact-fallback dialog shipped with a "Call" button that called `launchUrl` directly with no failure handling or `LaunchMode` — every other `launchUrl` call site in the app (`about_screen.dart`, `license_screen.dart`) awaits it, checks the returned bool, and shows an error SnackBar on failure with `mode: LaunchMode.externalApplication`; fixed to match. Also directly verified (not just documented as an assumption) that #273's "trust only the last `X-Forwarded-For` hop" design is safe against spoofing: curled the live `storefront` function with forged `X-Forwarded-For` headers (`8.8.4.4`, `1.1.1.1`) and confirmed via the response headers that Cloudflare fronts every Supabase Edge Function call (`server: cloudflare`) — Cloudflare's own docs confirm it re-appends the true connecting IP to `X-Forwarded-For` before the request reaches the function, and that value cannot be overridden by a client-supplied header even via Cloudflare's own Transform Rules. So a forged header cannot bypass the block-list or rate limit. One inconclusive side-observation from the same test (6 rapid calls to `submit_license_request` never tripped its 5-per-10-min limit) is most likely this sandbox's outbound NAT rotating egress IPs per request, not a rate-limiter bug — not chased further; worth re-checking from a single stable IP if it recurs. `flutter analyze` clean, all 705 tests pass. Result: FIFO cost-basis and refund-as-append-only-reversal were already fully built (the doc's proposed `cost_layers`/`ReturnEvent` designs were redundant — `StockLots`/`rebuildStockLots` already does exactly the "replay on sync-order mismatch" the doc worried about), and the sync model is an outbox + last-write-wins over Drift tables, not an event stream. Cash Register's `computeExpectedCash` and Payment Accounts' `computeAccountBalance` already iterate `Payments` filtered by method, so a split sale's cash-vs-wallet portions attribute correctly with zero changes to either. No schema/migration/sync-mapper changes needed — `Payments` was already a fully synced table. New tests in `sales_repository_test.dart` (split finalize, split refund mirroring, the change-given refund regression) and `payment_labels_test.dart`; `flutter analyze` clean, **668/668 tests pass**. **Live-verified on the iOS Simulator** (not the owner's device): built + ran the app, added an item, opened Checkout, picked Split, entered Cash 300 + AYAPay 400 against a 700 total, watched the "Remaining" indicator go from a red −300/−300 to green 0 as amounts were edited and Save enable/disable accordingly, confirmed the sale, saw the invoice list net the day's total correctly, opened the invoice (this is where the `invoice_view.dart` label bug was actually caught), refunded it, and confirmed the day's invoice total netted back to 0 Ks with a `RFD-...` "Refunded" row — the whole flow end-to-end, not just unit tests. **Installed to the owner's iPhone same session** (`flutter run --release -d 00008150-001A44C41E08401C --dart-define-from-file=env.local.json --dart-define=COMMERCE_UI=true`, Xcode build 57.4s, installed + launched cleanly, run process then stopped — app stays installed). Extracted all six into top-level functions parameterized on an explicit `CashSession session` instead of `widget.session` (`showCashReportSheet` is the new public entry point) — `reportFor`/`buildCashSessionReportPdf`/`buildCashSessionReportCsv`/`CashSessionReportFormatter` already handled a null `closedAt`/`closingAmount`/`variance` gracefully, so nothing needed to change there, only where the sheet could be opened from. `_OpenCard` gained a new "Cash Session Report" `OutlinedButton` (between the expected-cash row and "Close register") wired to the same `showCashReportSheet`. `flutter analyze` clean, **678/678 tests pass** (pure refactor, no pure-logic change — no new tests needed). **Live-verified on the iOS Simulator**: opened the register, tapped the new "Cash Session Report" button, got the same Print/Share PDF/Share CSV sheet a closed session already had, and "Share PDF" produced a valid 9 KB PDF via the real share sheet — the owner can now audit what's driving the expected-cash figure at any time, not only after locking the session shut. No schema/migration change. |

| 2026-08-27 | 264 | **Checkout sheet: the numeric pad wouldn't collapse when the seller opened "Add customer," and "Add customer" itself could get permanently stuck NOT auto-appearing on a credit/shortfall sale — both from the same `_padVisible`/`_customerSectionLatched` interaction, found while investigating an owner report of the pad "following" them as they scrolled.** **(1)** The pinned numeric pad (`checkout_sheet.dart:798-809`, deliberately outside the scroll per #224 so it never falls below the fold) had no code path that ever closed it when the seller tapped "Add customer" open — it stayed pinned, eating half the sheet's height, for as long as `_padVisible` was true regardless of whether the amount field was still relevant. `_customerSection`'s `onTap` now sets `_padVisible = false` whenever it expands. **(2)** `_customerSectionLatched` (the "auto-show Add customer on a credit/shortfall sale" flag) was gated on `midAmountEntry = _padVisible || _paid.text.trim().isNotEmpty` — but `_paid.text` never re-empties itself just because the pad closes, so once ANY amount had been typed, the latch condition (`forced && !midAmountEntry`) could never fire again for the rest of the sheet's life: a seller who typed a shortfall amount got the customer section stuck collapsed permanently, with no automatic recovery — they had to scroll up and tap it open by hand every time. | 2026-08-22 | 216 | **Wi-Fi printer discovery found nothing on iPhones — the scan was walking the CELLULAR subnet; interface selection now prefers Wi-Fi/ethernet and skips cellular/VPN, plus probe timeout raised and a denied-permission hint.** The owner reported "Printers on this Wi-Fi" always empty even with the printer on the same network (users can't be expected to type IPs). The /24 TCP-probe scan itself existed (`scanNetworkPrinters`, 32-parallel batches on port 9100) and iOS's `NSLocalNetworkUsageDescription` was declared — the real bug: `lanScanPlan` took the FIRST IPv4 address on the phone, and an iPhone with mobile data on lists `pdp_ip0` (cellular) before `en0` (Wi-Fi), so the scan walked the carrier's subnet where the printer can never be. `lanScanPlan` now takes named interfaces, prefers `en*`/`wlan*`/`eth*`, never scans through `pdp*`/`rmnet`/`ccinet`/`*wwan*` or `utun*`/`tap*`/`tun*` VPN tunnels, and skips every local address (the phone itself can't list as a printer); probe timeout 400→700 ms for congested shop Wi-Fi. The empty result no longer dead-ends either — a new `printerWifiDeniedHint` (EN+MY) under it points at iOS Settings > Privacy & Security > Local Network allow + same-network requirement, covering the other two real-world causes (denied OS permission on first prompt, AP-isolated guest Wi-Fi). Pure logic, 4 new lanScanPlan cases (cellular-vs-wifi preference, VPN skip, non-lan fallback, loopback-only) in printer_connection_test.dart. `flutter analyze` clean for touched files, **586/586 tests pass**. Deployed to the owner's iPhone for live verification against their PT210L. |

PDF sizes stay A4/A5 deliberately (the only formats used here; more would be decision fatigue, not completeness). | 2026-08-25 | 247 | **Wi-Fi test print printed non-stop on an Epson, and every printer/paper choice becomes a GoodCashier-style tap-the-arrow picker.** Owner reported both: pressing Test print on a Wi-Fi Epson made paper keep coming out continuously, and they want printer type + paper type chosen from a drop-down list (arrow → ordered list → pick), not segmented buttons. **(1) Continuous-print root cause:** `sendNetworkBytes` tore the socket down with `destroy()` (RST) a fixed 200 ms after `flush()` — flush only guarantees the OS send buffer took the bytes, so a large raster job could still be mid-flight when the RST landed. Epson-class printers treat the aborted connection as a comm error and their auto-reprint behavior then re-emits whatever is in the buffer — one tap, endless copies. The transport now prepends `ESC @` (initializes the head state, wiping any half-received job left by an earlier abort), then closes GRACEFULLY (`await socket.close()`, proper FIN) with a 5 s timeout falling back to a hard teardown so a wedged printer can never hang the UI — every byte is acknowledged before we hang up, which is exactly what `esc_pos_printer`-style raw-9100 clients do. Second hardening layer: `PrinterService._send` now runs jobs through a serial `_jobQueue` future chain (failures don't wedge it) — Wi-Fi printers are single-byte-stream devices, and two concurrent jobs (checkout auto-print racing a manual Test print) could previously interleave in the printer's buffer and produce garbled/repeated output even with correct per-job framing; Bluetooth/USB writes ride the same queue harmlessly. **(2) Picker UI:** new shared `lib/features/printing/picker_field.dart` — `AppPickerField<T>` renders like an outlined text field (small label over the current value, trailing drop arrow); tapping opens `showAppPickerSheet` via the existing `showAppModal` (bottom sheet on phones, dialog on tablets): title, optional hint, options in order, green check on the current pick, tap to choose. Applied everywhere the settings screens used `SegmentedButton`: connection type (Bluetooth/Wi-Fi/USB with icons, same scan/auto-load side effects on switch), thermal paper size 58/80 mm (per-printer scoping logic untouched), PDF document size A4/A5 (hint text kept under the field), label size 40×30/50×30/50×40, and the one-time "what paper does this printer use?" pairing prompt (now the same sheet instead of a dialog-with-segmented-buttons). No new i18n keys (every label already existed). **Ripple-effect check:** `sendNetworkBytes` has exactly one caller (`PrinterService._send`, now `_sendNow` behind the queue); no table/column/SettingsRepository key/provider touched, so no multi-shop or stale-watch surface; `PrinterTransportSection`'s public API unchanged (both settings screens re-verified); remaining SegmentedButtons in analytics/expenses/inventory/onboarding are unrelated features, deliberately left alone. `flutter analyze` clean, **623/623 tests pass**. App-only, rebuilt to the owner's iPhone. |
| 2026-08-25 | 246 | **Renewal receipt: a shop that pays now gets a number it can quote and a link it can come back to — plus the first licence-expiry reminder the app has ever had.** Owner asked for an invoice so a shop has "something to follow". What it had was a bare 36-character UUID on the /renew confirmation screen and nothing else: close the tab and there was no way to learn whether the payment had been accepted, so the only recourse was a Viber message. **Migration 0069** adds `invoice_no` (unique, backfilled), `payment_status` (default `manual`, so every existing row and the whole admin Confirm/Decline flow is untouched), `mmpay_order_id`, `mmpay_expires_at`, `paid_at`, and a partial index on `payment_status='paid' AND status='pending'`. `invoice_no` is `INV-` + the row id's first 8 hex — derived from the id rather than a sequence or timestamp, so no extra table and no collision between two requests submitted in the same millisecond, exactly the reasoning `submit_order`'s `order_no` already uses. **The MMPay columns ship now on purpose**: `invoice_no` is what we will hand the gateway as its `orderId`, so the number a shop quotes on Viber, the row here, and the order in MMPay's own dashboard all reconcile on ONE number — passing a UUID instead would leave that dashboard unmatchable. `payment_status` is deliberately a separate axis from `status`: one tracks the money, the other the licence, and a webhook can advance the first while the second is still pending (payment taken, minting crashed) — a state the shop must be able to see rather than sit in the dark about, and the admin must be able to find. **New `receipt` action** on the `storefront` Edge Function returns a hand-picked column subset for one request id. Keyed by the id alone, which is a server-generated UUIDv4 handed only to the submitting browser — the ordinary order-tracking-link model — and the one sensitive field it can return, `issued_key`, is device-bound at activation, so a leaked link is not a usable licence. `payment_proof_path` (a photo of someone's bank app) is never returned. No RLS change: `license_requests` stays service-role only and the Edge Function holds that role. **New `RenewalReceiptView`** replaces the bare-UUID confirmation and doubles as the `/renew?receipt=<id>` entry point — one widget, two ways in — showing status, the line items, the licence key once fulfilled, the decline reason when rejected (which is what actually removes the "why was mine refused?" messages), copy-link, and print via the browser's own dialog (`web.window.print()`, matching `storefront_download.dart`'s web-only pattern — no PDF dependency, and every browser's print dialog offers Save as PDF). Admin's Requests tab and its confirm dialog both gained a copyable `Invoice` field, and a `Paid · needs key` pill for the reconciliation case that otherwise renders identically to an ordinary pending row. **Expiry reminders**, the second half: `LicenseExpiryWatcher` (mirrors `ReferralWatcher` — launch + resume + 6h timer, gated on `Env.hasBackend` like its neighbours, which also keeps app-lifetime timers out of widget tests) fires a local notification at 7/3/1/0 days. The decision is a **pure function**, `computeExpiryReminder`, per this project's rule that logic like this lives in plain Dart with tests — 12 of them, covering the Free plan (never warned: Free is the plan, not a lapse), already-lapsed (silent — the Sell banner already says so, and a notification there is scolding), and calendar-day counting so an 23:00 expiry reads as "0 days left" like the banner. The watermark is `<expiresAt>|<threshold>`, **shop-scoped via `_shopKey`**: a device can switch shops without `wipeSyncedData()` clearing `AppSettings`, so a device-global key would let shop A's "already warned" silence shop B — 3 new isolation tests in `settings_repository_test.dart` pin that. Embedding the expiry in the watermark also makes it self-invalidating: renewing moves the date, the stored value stops matching, and the next cycle warns again with no reset code anywhere. The notification names no price and no way to pay — a store build has no purchase UI to send anyone to, and a notification is as much a call to action as a button. **Ripple-effect check**: `license_requests` is server-only (not a Drift table) so no `schemaVersion`/sync-mapper work; `list_requests` uses `select("*")` and picked the new columns up for free; `fulfill_request`/`reject_request` don't touch them. The check also turned up **`LicenseRequestService` (`lib/features/license/license_request.dart`) as dead code with zero callers** — a leftover direct anon insert that bypasses the Edge Function's validation, rate limit, honeypot and now `invoice_no` stamping; flagged for removal rather than deleted in this change-set. Separately, the admin console's "Expiring in 7 days" overview card, `AdminShopFilter.expiring`, and the per-shop Viber action **already existed** — checked before building, so nothing was duplicated there. 10 new i18n keys, en+my. `flutter analyze` clean; **623 tests pass** (608 + 12 reminder + 3 isolation). **Not yet deployed** — migration 0069 and the `storefront` function need `supabase db push` + `functions deploy` before the receipt link resolves in production. |

Guideline 3.1.1 bans "buttons, external links, or other calls to action that direct customers to purchasing mechanisms other than in-app purchase" — it judges the *button*, not whether the destination happens to be a web page, so the in-code note at `license_screen.dart` claiming a plain web link is out of scope was wrong. The 2025 Epic injunction lifted that ban **only on the United States storefront**; Myanmar, the actual market, still has it in full. The store build therefore has to carry no commerce whatsoever and qualify under **3.1.3(b) Multiplatform Services** instead (service exists on Android + web, user redeems a licence they already hold, nothing sold in the iOS app) — the Square/Shopify/Toast pattern. **New `lib/core/build_flags.dart`: `kCommerceUiEnabled = bool.fromEnvironment('COMMERCE_UI', defaultValue: false)`.** The default is false **on purpose** — fail closed, so a store build that forgets the define is still compliant rather than newly in violation. Only the direct-install APK opts in, with an explicit `--dart-define=COMMERCE_UI=true`; deliberately NOT added to `env.local.json`, since that file is passed to the App Store build too and would switch commerce back on in exactly the build that must not have it (documented in the flag's dartdoc and in the `deploy` skill). **Gated when off:** `_PurchasePaths` drops both purchase paths and the "Buy or renew Premium" heading, keeping only a neutral "Premium licensing" notice plus the Check-for-renewal button (re-checking a licence you already hold is not commerce — it is the whole point of 3.1.3(b)); the never-activated screen's commerce-only tail is dropped divider and all, rather than left as a heading with nothing actionable under it; `premiumUpgradeCta` ("Upgrade") becomes `premiumManageLicenseCta` ("Manage license") through a new shared `upgradeCtaLabel()` in `premium_gate.dart`, so `PremiumGate`, `showPremiumRequiredDialog` and Settings' two `_LockedTile`s can't drift apart; Sell's expiry banner drops its "— tap to renew" tail; the Help guide's Settings section swaps in a variant without the "tap Upgrade, contact Support for a key" instructions; **Refer & earn is hidden entirely** — it quotes the monthly price, pays commission when a referred shop *pays*, and redeems that balance for licence days, so the Settings entrance is hidden AND `ReferralScreen.build` guards itself, since hiding only the entrance leaves the screen one future `push` away from resurrection. **Untouched in every build** (correctly — none of it is commerce): licence-key entry, Check for renewal, the free trial, and `licenseGetKey` ("the key you received when you subscribed" describes access to something already purchased, which 3.1.3(b) explicitly permits). **New `test/commerce_ui_gate_test.dart`** pins the two ways this dies quietly: the default flipping to true, and a stray commerce string reappearing. The third test scans `lib/` and asserts every use of a commerce-only l10n getter lives in a file that also consults `kCommerceUiEnabled` — rendering the real License screen would need the whole Drift/Supabase stack, and a source scan survives file moves and new gated call sites. It earned its keep immediately: it caught `referral_screen.dart` guarding only its entrance, which is why the second guard above exists. 6 new i18n keys, en+my (`licenseManagedElsewhereTitle`/`Body`, `licenseAlreadyLicensedTitle`, `premiumManageLicenseCta`, `helpGuideSettingsBodyNoCommerce`, `licenseExpiringSoonNeutral`). `flutter analyze` clean; **608 tests pass** (605 + 3 new). The filter switches itself off when the shop doesn't track stock (its control is hidden then; a filter you can no longer see must not keep hiding rows) — provider-tested incl. that wedge case. **(3) Per-product history** — it lived only inside the product editor, so "when did this move?" meant open-editor → find-history → back every time: the row's overflow menu gains "Stock history" (opens the existing per-product `StockHistoryScreen`), and the shop-wide Stock Movements screen gains a product-name filter field (`movementProductSearchProvider` + debounced mirror, wired into `filteredMovementsProvider` and the empty-state's "show all" reset). **(4) Web-order payment badge** — the storefront confirmation built its `InvoiceData` without `paymentStatus`, so `InvoiceView`'s default stamped EVERY order red "အကြွေးကျန်" (unpaid/credit): a customer who had just sent a transfer screenshot read that they owe money, and a COD order was never credit at all. `invoicePaymentStatusDisplay` gains two pre-sale stages — `cod_pending` → neutral "Pay on delivery"/"ပစ္စည်းရောက်မှ ပေးချေရမည်", `transfer_pending` → attention "Awaiting confirmation"/"အတည်ပြုရန် ကျန်" — the storefront passes the right one per method, and `isPendingOrderPaymentStatus` suppresses the "amount due" row for them (the total already says the figure; restating it beside a pending note reads like a debt notice). Sales rows can never carry the new codes (they are 'unpaid'|'partial'|'paid' by construction), so in-app invoices and PDFs are byte-identical. **Verification:** `flutter analyze` clean, **600/600 tests pass** (14 new: compactMoneyLabel ×4, barFillAlpha ×2, web-order display stages ×4, low-stock filter provider ×4). Ripple check: `filteredProductsProvider` readers = inventory list + CSV export (export respecting the filter is the point); purchase-order editor deliberately uses its own unfiltered source (documented in place); `filteredMovementsProvider` single reader; no table/schema/settings changes. App rebuild to iPhone + storefront web redeploy in this change-set. |

Diagnosed by content, not title: both web projects share `web/index.html`, so the `<title>` is identical — grepping the live `main.dart.js` instead found 2 occurrences of "All In One POS Admin" and zero storefront strings, proving the production deployment of `allinonepos-shop` was built from `lib/admin/admin_main.dart` (a deploy mixup — the admin build went to the wrong Vercel project at some earlier session; no code regression, nothing in the repo changed). Lesson for future deploys: the §3/§3b deploy steps are identical except the target + project, and `vercel deploy` from a `build/web` cwd silently publishes to whatever that directory was LAST linked to — always `vercel link --project <name>` immediately before `vercel deploy` for web targets, and grep the deployed `main.dart.js` for an entry-point-unique string as the post-deploy check (a title check can't catch this class of mistake). |

| 2026-08-23 | 233 | **QA audit Critical fixes: order-conversion COGS + cross-shop cart leak.** Full source-level QA/edge-case audit (scratch proofs in `test_scratch_qa/`, findings logged separately) surfaced 2 Criticals; both fixed. **(C1) Refunding an order-converted sale re-entered the returned units into FIFO stock at COST ZERO, permanently overstating profit by their full real cost.** Root cause chain: `OrdersRepository.convertToSale` wrote `SaleItems` with NO `costSnapshot` and its private `_recordStockOut` recorded only a movement + cached-level decrement (never consuming lots — unlike `SalesRepository._recordStockOut`); refunding such a sale found `costSnapshot == null` → reopened no lot while stamping the return movement `unit_cost = 0`; the next `consumeStockLots` detected the ledger/lots mismatch, rebuilt from the ledger, and the rebuild opened the returned units as a lot at cost 0 — every later sale of them then booked COGS 0 (proven by scratch test qa2: 4-unit sale cost 18,000 instead of 24,000 @6,000). Two-sided fix: (a) `convertToSale` now consumes FIFO lots oldest-first (flat-cost fallback for shortfall), stamps the weighted-average movement `unitCost`, returns the real total cost into `costSnapshot` — order-channel sales now carry the same accounting as Sell-screen checkouts, fixing the channel profit inconsistency too; (b) `SalesRepository.refundSale` heals LEGACY rows already written without a costSnapshot: when the line has no snapshot but its sale DID record a stock-out movement (new one-shot probe, which also keeps invoice-only shops' refunds lot-free by construction), the lot reopens at the product's current `costPrice` instead of nothing — strictly better than the 0-cost poison, and free-text lines (`productId == ''`) can never mint ghost lots again. **(C2) Branch/account shop switches leaked the old shop's in-memory Sell state into the new shop.** `onShopDbSwap` only reopened the database file — cart, held carts, Sell search text and category filter are device-global providers, so Shop A's cart survived into Shop B, where checkout either failed confusingly or (invoice-only shops, whose checkout never looks products up) committed sale rows referencing ANOTHER SHOP's product ids. New `resetSellSessionState(ref)` (`lib/features/sell/sell_session_reset.dart`) clears all four surfaces; wired into BOTH swap hooks (`branch_providers.dart` switchBranch, `account_providers.dart` wipe-and-claim AND shop-promotion paths) plus new `HeldSalesNotifier.clearAll()`. **Verification:** `flutter analyze` clean (9 pre-existing warnings unchanged), **571/571 tests pass** (6 new in `test/orders_conversion_cogs_test.dart`: conversion stamps real costSnapshot + consumes lots; refunded conversion restores at original cost — consume-everything costs exactly 5×6000; legacy no-snapshot rows heal at costPrice, return movement stamped 6000 not 0; free-text lines never mint ghost `""` lots; and `test/sell_session_reset_test.dart`: reset clears cart/held/search/category, clearAll keeps hold/resume usable). Remaining from the audit's High tier, NOT fixed here (deliberately scoped to the two Criticals): barcode-scan path bypassing the grid's stock cap (qa1 proof), convert-to-sale double-tap race (qa3 proof) — both still reproducible via their scratch tests. No schema/migration/Edge Function change — purely client-side repositories/providers; app rebuild to device required, nothing to deploy server-side. |

Residual known limit: concurrent same-row edits still resolve LWW by device clock (hybrid clocks = future work); one-time cost: every device's first post-migration pull re-fetches its shop history once (LWW-guarded, safe). **H3:** `analyticsSummaryProvider` (P&L/Owner's Equity downstream) now also watches an invalidation-only `_saleItemChangesProvider` — sale_items arrive after their parent sale during pulls, so profit could stay stale indefinitely. **H4:** `consumeStockLots` checks Σ(lots) against Σ(movement deltas) before consuming and replays the full movement ledger (`rebuildStockLots`, chronological + rowid tiebreak, mirroring the live write paths) on mismatch — heals foreign pulled movements, fresh installs, and restores at the exact moment COGS matters; documented trade-off: products with past oversells permanently differ by their untracked units and re-replay each consume (idempotent, bounded, correct-over-cheap). Tests: new stock_levels_outbox_test.dart (creation pushes once; adjust/sell push none; reorder edit pushes), stock_lots_heal_test.dart (pulled-movement replay prices COGS from real lots not fallback; fresh-install full replay; consistent cache untouched — same lot seq survives), backup_test covers dual-domain cursor clearing; sales_repository refund test updated to assert stock_levels ABSENCE. Ripple: no other stock_levels enqueuers remain (grep-verified); realtime subscription table list unaffected. `flutter analyze` clean (1 pre-existing dart:html info), **523/523 tests pass**, migration pushed to prod and column verified live via REST. |
| 2026-08-22 | 215 | **CRITICAL sync data-loss fix: `_reconcileUpsertsAlreadyRemote` dropped every pending offline EDIT to an already-synced row (found by the architecture audit, introduced #93-era self-healing in commit a83ad02).** The reconcile step ran at the start of every `syncNow`, fetched the full remote table for any table with pending outbox upserts, and dropped any outbox row whose **id existed remotely — comparing ids only, never timestamps**. Since every already-synced row's id of course exists remotely from its original creation push, this silently destroyed every subsequent edit to a mutable row (order status moves, product price/name edits, customer/supplier changes, cash-session closes, recurring-expense pauses) at the next sync: local showed the new value while cloud + other devices kept the old one forever — silent divergence, invisible to analyze/tests because the two existing "dropped when remote already has the row" tests only covered identical-content duplicates. **Fix:** a pending upsert is now dropped as "already remote" ONLY when the remote copy's `updated_at` is strictly NEWER than the local row's (`_remoteNewerThanLocal` helper; equal timestamps also keep the row — Drift stores seconds, so a same-second edit carries an identical timestamp with different content; re-pushing is idempotent, dropping is not). Remote-fetch failure during reconcile keeps all rows and lets the normal push decide. The same id-only flaw in `_push`'s RLS/unique-violation heal path was fixed with the same rule (it could drop a legitimate edit that merely failed for a stale-JWT reason; such rows now stay queued for session-refresh heal / force-apply). Rode along: successful pushes now clear the row's `dirty` flag (documented meaning "changes not yet pushed" — it previously stayed true forever after a successful push; no query reads it, write-only metadata). Tests: 3 new regression cases in sync_engine_test.dart — offline edit to synced row IS pushed (fails on old code); pending edit OLDER than remote is dropped + pull converges (the only droppable case); fetch-failure during reconcile keeps + pushes the edit; the 2 duplicate-outbox tests renamed to their real post-fix semantics (idempotent re-push, not blind drop). Ripple-effect checked: no query anywhere filters on `dirty` (grep-verified); branch-switch drain (`drainForSwitch`) now sees honest pending counts instead of ones shrunk by silent drops. Remaining audit findings (stock_levels absolute push H1, clock-skew watermark H2, analytics missing sale_items watch H3, FIFO lot replay H4) recorded separately, unfixed. New i18n keys: none. `flutter analyze` clean (1 pre-existing dart:html info), **518/518 tests pass**. |

| 2026-08-17 | 160 | **First real interactive test pass of the mobile app — not just `flutter analyze`/`flutter test` — found and fixed 2 bugs neither of those would ever catch.** Asked directly whether testing had actually been done device-by-device; honest answer was no — every prior mobile fix this session had been verified by static analysis + the owner's own bug reports, never by actually opening the app and clicking through it. Built and ran the app in the iOS Simulator in **debug mode** (so Flutter framework errors print instead of being silently swallowed, same technique that cracked #159), then clicked through onboarding end-to-end, Sell (add to cart, checkout, confirm sale), Inventory (list, add product with sale price), Orders/Invoices (confirmed the sale posted correctly, stock decremented 24→23), Analytics (Premium-lock screen), and Settings (License, and 20+ other rows) in both English and Myanmar. **Bug 1**: the onboarding "Your shop" page showed a real `RenderFlex overflowed by 30 pixels` debug banner across the Back/Next button row in English. Root cause: `FilledButtonThemeData`'s `minimumSize: Size.fromHeight(52)` sets width to `double.infinity` (a `Size.fromHeight` gotcha — fine for the app's normal full-width buttons), and this particular button was wrapped in a `maxWidth: 220` `ConstrainedBox`, so the infinite-width demand resolved to a forced 220pt button regardless of its actual "Next" text — overflowing once page dots + a "Back" button shared the same row on a 402pt-wide phone. Confirmed via the debug console's exact stack trace (`onboarding_flow.dart:89`), not guesswork. Fixed by giving the button an explicit finite `minimumSize` instead of the maxWidth cap, so it sizes to content; verified via hot reload (`kill -SIGUSR1` on the running `flutter run` process) showing the banner gone and the button correctly content-sized. **Bug 2**: every fresh Free-plan shop showed a permanent amber "License expires in 0 days — tap to renew" banner on the Sell screen — directly contradicting the onboarding copy's own promise that Free "works forever, no card, no signup, no key needed." Root cause: `computeLicenseStatus` (`license_status.dart`) deliberately keeps `expiresAt` as a non-null placeholder (`now`) for the Free plan so `kind` can still be `active` — `license_widgets.dart` already had a `plan != LicensePlan.free` guard for exactly this reason, but `sell_screen.dart`'s own `expiringSoon` calculation read `status.expiresAt` without that same guard, so `daysLeft` was always `0` and the warning fired unconditionally for every Free-plan device. Fixed by adding the identical guard; grepped every other reader of `.expiresAt` in the codebase (ripple-effect check) and confirmed no other site had the same gap. Also investigated two things that looked alarming but weren't real bugs: the Inventory tab showing 6 pre-existing "real" products immediately after choosing "Continue Free" on a supposedly brand-new shop turned out to be `DemoSeed` (`lib/data/repositories/demo_seed.dart`) — an intentional, pre-existing starter-catalog feature, lazily triggered only when Inventory is first opened (confirmed by reading the actual on-disk SQLite files directly, not assumption); and a Drift "database opened multiple times" warning during the Free-plan shop-switch cutover was traced to the documented, intentional `DatabaseSession.reopenForShop` mechanism, not a data-isolation leak. `flutter analyze` clean, **406/406 tests pass**. Not yet rebuilt to the owner's physical device this session (Simulator-verified only) — a real ripple-effect-check limitation of this pass: the fixes are proven correct by direct testing, but production deployment to the owner's phone is a separate next step. |
| 2026-08-17 | 159 | **Storefront web: fixed a Flutter framework-level layout crash that made every product card's price and Add-to-cart button invisible and untappable.** The owner reported this directly with a screenshot, right after #158's session-repair fix unblocked publishing for the first time — the digits of each price were wrapping one-per-line ("7\n0\n0"), and the Add button couldn't be tapped at all. Release web builds swallow Flutter framework layout exceptions silently (the failing subtree just renders nothing, invisible under the ancestor `Card`'s `Clip.antiAlias`), so the real error only surfaced by running the storefront locally in **debug** mode (`flutter run -d web-server`), which printed the actual crash: `BoxConstraints forces an infinite width` inside `FilledButton`'s internal Material sizing (`button_style_button.dart:698`). Root cause: `_ProductCard`'s info `Column` used `crossAxisAlignment: start`, which forces the `Column` to query its price/button `Row` child for its *intrinsic* width to size itself — an operation that can't be honestly answered when that `Row` contains an `Expanded` (an `Expanded`'s width is only defined by real constraint-based layout, not some inherent "ideal" size), so the intrinsic-width query crashed the moment it reached the `FilledButton`. A first attempt at just switching `crossAxisAlignment` to `stretch` alone was tested and confirmed **not** sufficient — same crash, same stack trace, only the line number moved (ruling out stale build/cache as the reason). The actual fix: wrapped the `FilledButton.tonal`/qty-stepper `Row` in `IntrinsicWidth`, forcing a real two-pass measurement instead of letting the ambient Flex layout hand it unbounded constraints; kept the `stretch` change too since it's a correct simplification (the `Text` children don't depend on `start` specifically — they already render left-anchored via their own default `textAlign`). A follow-on cosmetic issue then appeared — `IntrinsicWidth`'s greedy sizing pushed the button to its full natural width (Myanmar label + default Material padding), over-squeezing the price down to a truncated "7…" in the ~170px card — fixed by shrinking the price text to `titleSmall` and giving the button a compact `FilledButton.styleFrom` (tighter padding, `minimumSize: Size(0, 36)`, `labelMedium` text). Verified in three stages: local debug preview (fresh browser tab, zero console errors, price/button render correctly at mobile viewport), `flutter test` (**406/406 pass**, unchanged), and the real production deploy at `allinonepos-shop.vercel.app/caesar-shop-45b3` (fresh tab, service-worker/cache cleared first, zero console errors, price and Add button both render correctly). Automated tap-simulation on the Add button did not register a click in either the local debug preview or the production check — assessed as a known limitation of clicking into Flutter web's canvas/semantics layer under headless automation (likely needs the accessibility layer activated first), not a product bug, since the layout crash that made the button genuinely untappable is conclusively fixed and no console error accompanies the click attempt; flagged to the owner to verify tapping "Add" directly on their own device. `flutter analyze` clean. Deployed: storefront web rebuilt and redeployed to Vercel production. |

Checked `auth.refresh_tokens` for the affected user directly: **zero successful refreshes in over 24 hours**, spanning multiple app relaunches including the one that shipped #150's own retry logic — conclusive proof `refreshSession()` has never once succeeded on this device, which only fits one explanation: the local refresh token itself is missing, so the call throws before any network round-trip, and no number of retries (however well-implemented) can ever reach the server. | 2026-08-17 | 149 | **Root cause of the recurring Storefront/session failures found: the self-serve trial's periodic session-refresh heartbeat was dead code from a string typo, so a JWT that missed its `shop_id` claim once had no way to ever self-heal.** The owner reported #147/#148's fix didn't actually resolve Storefront publish (still showing the new `storefrontSessionStale` message) and that Shop Login's "Register" also started failing with "Activate this device first" — asked for an overall check rather than another one-off patch. Traced it directly against the real backend rather than guessing: `licenses`/`auth.users` both correctly carry `shop_id: shop-fd9f88405a76` and an active trial (also confirmed the "admin extend not becoming active" report was a false alarm — `licenses.expires_at`/`updated_at` show the extend from #142/#143 *did* land server-side, Oct→Nov; almost certainly just the admin dashboard not having been reloaded after the action). But `auth.refresh_tokens` showed **zero refresh activity since the trial was originally minted** on 2026-08-16, despite two full app relaunches since — meaning the client's JWT has been running on its original, aging token this whole time with no successful refresh, on any of the 3 attempts (the periodic reverify, or either of #147/#148's retry-on-RLS-failure calls). Found why: `LicenseController.refreshOnline()` — the app's *only* periodic "is my session still good" heartbeat (runs once at every launch + every 6h) — exempts trial/free/offline-token licenses from re-verification with `if (lic.key == 'FREE-TRIAL' || ...)`, but `LicenseRepository.startFreeTrial()` actually caches the key as plain `'TRIAL'` — `'FREE-TRIAL'` is a string nothing in the codebase has ever set (confirmed by grep). So every single heartbeat for a self-serve trial shop fell through to `_repo.activate('TRIAL')` instead of skipping — a call guaranteed to fail server-side (no license row has literal key `'TRIAL'`) — and since that failure happens *before* `activate()`'s own success-path `refreshSession()` call, the heartbeat has been silently doing nothing useful for trial shops since the self-serve trial feature (#139) shipped. Fixed the key check (`'TRIAL'`), and — since this exemption branch is also the app's best periodic opportunity to repair a JWT that never picked up its claim — added an explicit `refreshSession()` call specifically for the trial case, so the very next launch (or up-to-6h periodic tick) gets a real chance to self-heal instead of only doing so reactively inside a failing write (`StorefrontRepository._withRlsRetry` from #147 still stays as a second layer of defense). `flutter analyze` clean, **404/404 tests pass** unchanged (pure control-flow fix, no test exercised this specific string comparison — confirmed via grep before concluding none needed updating). Deployed: mobile app rebuilt to the owner's iPhone — next launch should finally refresh the stuck session and unblock both Storefront publish and Shop Login Register. |

This is a known error class in this codebase already (`lib/data/sync/outbox_error.dart`'s `OutboxErrorClass.rls42501`, used by the outbox push loop for a different self-heal path) — reused its string-based `classifyOutboxError()` rather than inventing a second classifier. Fix: `StorefrontRepository` (`lib/features/storefront/storefront_repository.dart`) gained a `_withRlsRetry<T>` wrapper — on a `42501` error, force `auth.refreshSession()` once and retry the operation exactly once before giving up; applied to `publish()` (split into `publish()`/`_publishImpl()` so the retry re-runs the whole create-or-reenable flow, not just the last write), `updateProfile()`, and `setEnabled()`, the three RLS-scoped write paths on this table. Self-healing rather than a one-off patch: this fixes the reported bug regardless of the exact moment the session went stale, and the same pattern is available to reapply to any other RLS-scoped write path that shows the same symptom. Deliberately scoped to just `StorefrontRepository` rather than a codebase-wide interceptor — see the discussion opened directly after this entry about generic error messages more broadly. `flutter analyze` clean. Phases 1-3 (daily gate, onboarding, ripple cleanup) are logic-and-UI-only and were live-verified on iPhone 17 Pro to the extent the session's iOS Simulator tooling allowed; Phase 4 (Free→Premium data migration) is the one phase carrying genuine production risk — a real-device staging test is strongly recommended before it ships; Phase 5 (offline license fallback) touches Edge Functions that are written and type-checked but not yet deployed — see the `deploy` skill. |
| 2026-08-16 | 121 | **Phase 5 of the Online/Offline-mode retirement — universal offline-verifiable license fallback.** Before this phase, only a shop an admin manually hand-fulfilled via the admin console's `sign_offline` action ever got a cryptographically offline-verifiable `MMPOS1.` token; a normally key-activated or signed-up Premium shop had no fallback past the `activate` Edge Function's 7-day online grace window (`GRACE_DAYS`) if it lost connectivity for longer. New `supabase/functions/_shared/offline_token.ts` factors the existing Ed25519 signing logic (payload build, `b64url`/`hexToBytes`, `LICENSE_SIGNING_KEY_HEX` secret) out of `admin/index.ts`'s `sign_offline` case into a shared `signOfflineToken()`, called now from **two** places instead of one: the same admin action (unchanged behavior, just refactored — its two now-unused local helper functions and the now-unused `@noble/ed25519` import were removed from `admin/index.ts` rather than left dead) and, new, the `activate` Edge Function's default `activate` success path and `handleSignupShop` — both now mint a 1-month offline token best-effort (never blocks the activation itself if signing fails) and return it as `offline_token`/`offline_token_expires_at`. Client side: `LicenseRepository.activate()` caches the token via a new shop-scoped setting (`SettingsRepository.licenseOfflineFallbackToken`/`setLicenseOfflineFallbackToken`, key `license.offline_fallback.<shopId>` — deliberately *not* added to `isDeviceGlobalKey`, verified by grep before adding, since it must travel with Phase 4's `promoteShopIdentity` rekey). `LicenseController.refreshOnline()` (driven by the existing 6-hour silent re-verify) now falls back to `OfflineLicense.verify` on the cached token specifically when the Edge Function call fails with `network_error` — not on a genuine `invalid_key`/`device_mismatch`/`rate_limited`, which still fail normally. The fallback decision itself is factored into a new pure top-level function, `applyOfflineFallback` (extends `expiresAt` to the verified token's own expiry when that's later, never shortens it, returns null if the cached token is itself expired) — kept separate from the I/O (fetching the cached token, calling `OfflineLicense.verify`) specifically so it's unit-testable without Supabase/Riverpod plumbing, matching this project's "pure logic in plain Dart classes with unit tests" convention. Deliberately preserves the license's own `key`/`shopId`/`plan` rather than replacing them with the token's synthetic identity, so a later successful online re-verify still runs normally instead of this fallback silently pinning the shop to offline-only forever. `deno check` clean on all three touched/added `.ts` files (Deno installed via Homebrew specifically to verify this, since `flutter analyze` never touches Edge Functions) — two pre-existing `admin/index.ts` type errors unrelated to this change (a `body.carrier`/`body.id` typing gap on unrelated actions, confirmed via `git stash` against the pre-Phase-5 file) were left alone and flagged as a separate spawned cleanup task rather than fixed inline.
