| 2026-08-16 | 120 | **Phase 4 of the Online/Offline-mode retirement — Free-to-Premium shop-identity migration, the riskiest phase of the six.** A Free-plan shop's `shop_id` (`free-<deviceId>`) is synthesized purely client-side — no server row, no JWT claim (see `sync_providers.dart:95-105`'s `syncEngineProvider` comment). Before this phase, activating a key or signing up for a real account on such a shop would silently strand every local sale/product/customer behind the old Free identity while the license flipped to a brand-new server-assigned `shop_id`, since `LicenseController._reopenShopDbIfNeeded`/`DatabaseSession.reopenForShop` just opens (or creates empty) the target shop's file with no awareness that the *previous* file held real data that should travel with it. New `ShopDataTransitionService.promoteShopIdentity({fromShopId, toShopId})` (`lib/data/local/shop_data_transition_service.dart`) rewrites `shop_id` across all 23 synced tables (re-derived at implementation time via `grep 'with SyncColumns' tables.dart`, matching `wipeSyncedData`'s own enumeration minus `stock_lots`, which has no `shop_id` column — local-only FIFO cache) inside one Drift transaction, rekeys shop-scoped `app_settings` rows (`SettingsRepository._shopKey`'s `<base>.<shopId>` suffix), and enqueues an outbox `upsert` per surviving (non-deleted) row so the sync engine actually pushes the relabeled data — deliberately skipping backfill for already-deleted rows (pointless: nothing to reconcile for a shop that never existed server-side). **Deliberately idempotent by construction, not by an explicit "already done" flag**: every step is `WHERE shop_id = fromShopId`, so a table with nothing left at that id contributes zero rewritten rows and zero backfill on a second pass — this is the load-bearing property the crash-recovery design leans on. New `DatabaseSession.reopenForShopPromotedFrom({fromShopId, toShopId})` closes the current connection (renaming an open SQLite file is unsafe), renames the file (skipped if the target already exists — same idempotency), and reopens at the new path — used instead of the plain `reopenForShop` specifically for this case, so the promoted data's file travels with it rather than a blank file being created at the new id. **Crash recovery, since the file rename can't share a transaction with the data rewrite**: `SettingsRepository.pendingShopPromotion()` (new device-global key `shop.promote.pending`, `"<from>|<to>"`) is written *before* the rewrite starts and cleared only after the rename succeeds; new top-level `resolvePendingShopPromotion` runs at the very start of `DatabaseSession._openCutover` on every launch — if the marker is still set (a crash occurred), it safely re-runs the (idempotent) rewrite if the source file still exists, finishes the rename, and patches `license.json`'s `shop_id` to match, all before the normal cutover logic decides which file to open — otherwise `license.json` and the actual on-disk `shop_id` columns could disagree and every shop-scoped query would silently return zero rows. Wired into exactly the two places that actually mint a new server-side `shop_id` for an existing device — `LicenseController.activate()` (new `_promoteFreeShopIfNeeded`, before its existing `_reopenShopDbIfNeeded` call) and `AccountRepository.signupShop()` (new `_promoteFreeShopIfNeeded`, via a new `onShopPromoted` callback parallel to the existing `onShopDbSwap`). **`AccountRepository.createShopLogin` was in the original plan's file list but turned out, on closer reading, not to need this at all** — it adds an email/password login to the shop the device is *already* scoped to (no new `shop_id` minted), so there's no identity to promote; including it anyway would have been unnecessary risk in money/data-integrity-sensitive code. New tests: `shop_data_transition_service_test.dart` (rewrite + settings rekey + outbox backfill + deleted-row exclusion + idempotent re-run + no-op on empty/identical ids) and a new `shop_promotion_resume_test.dart` (three real file-system scenarios via a temp dir + a `path_provider` platform-channel mock, since `resolvePendingShopPromotion` uses the production `AppDatabase.forFile` path: no pending marker, crash before the rewrite committed, crash after the rename already completed). `flutter analyze` clean, **379/379 tests pass**. Not live-verified end-to-end on device (would require a real Supabase key/signup and is inherently destructive to test data) — correctness rests on the transaction/idempotency design, the file-system-level resume tests, and direct code review of both integration points. See `~/.claude/plans/sequential-scribbling-sifakis.md`; Phases 5-6 remain. |

This phase does the first, lowest-risk slice: decouples the daily gate from `operating.mode` entirely. `lib/features/onboarding/online_daily_gate.dart` renamed to `daily_gate.dart` (`OnlineDailyGate` → `DailyGate`, git-mv'd to preserve history); `onlineDailyGateNeededProvider` renamed `dailyGateNeededProvider` in `operating_mode_providers.dart`, dropping the `mode != 'online' → false` bailout — the trigger is now simply "has today's gate already run for this shop," for every shop regardless of plan or connectivity. The identity step gained a fully-offline path: `lib/features/staff/staff_ui.dart`'s previously-private `StaffModeCard._switchTo`/`_pickStaffMemberId` were extracted into shared top-level functions (`switchStaffRole`, `pickStaffMemberId`, `kSkipNamedStaffId`) so the daily gate can reuse the exact same local, SHA-256-hashed, zero-network staff-PIN machinery Settings already offers manually — no new auth primitive. A shop with a configured staff roster sees "Continue as Owner"/"Continue as Staff" tiles (with an "or sign in with an account" divider above the existing, unchanged email form, now explicitly optional rather than the only path); a single-owner shop with no roster (the common Free-plan case) auto-skips with zero added friction, matching the existing already-signed-in auto-skip. `lib/app.dart`'s `holdForDailyCheck` dropped its `isOnlineModeProvider` read so every install — not just previously-online-locked ones — resolves the gate before the shell shows. Two new i18n keys (`dailyGateContinueAsOwner`, `dailyGateContinueAsStaff`, `dailyGateOrSignIn`) in both `.arb`s. **A latent bug surfaced by making the gate universal, not introduced by this change**: `_detailsStep`'s `AccountRepository.currentAccountEmail` read was unguarded against an uninitialized Supabase instance — harmless while the gate only ever rendered for Online-mode shops in tests, but a hard crash the moment the gate became reachable from any test/app state; fixed with the same try/catch guard the file's pre-existing `_signedIn` getter already used for exactly this reason. Four widget-test files (`app_smoke_test.dart`, `orders_invoices_hub_test.dart`, `role_based_tabs_test.dart`, plus the new coverage in `operating_mode_test.dart`) needed a `dailyGateNeededProvider` override added, since their existing `setOperatingMode(offline)` setup previously (and only incidentally) doubled as "skip the daily gate" — now that the gate is universal, offline-tier test shops reach it too, exactly the "Offline-mode devices with a staff roster will see a mandatory prompt for the first time" behavior change flagged as a deliberate, called-out risk in the approved plan (see `~/.claude/plans/sequential-scribbling-sifakis.md`) rather than a side effect. Two new tests added directly (`dailyGateNeededProvider` triggers for a Free/offline-tier shop with no `operating.mode` ever set — previously inexpressible, since the old provider hard-returned `false` unless `mode == 'online'` — and confirms `false` once a gate is completed for today). `flutter analyze` clean, **372/372 tests pass**. Not yet live-verified on device — this phase's code paths (auto-skip for a signed-in or roster-empty shop) are exercised by the existing/extended automated tests; the new staff-roster branch (`_continueAsRole`) reuses `switchStaffRole` byte-for-byte from `StaffModeCard`, already proven live in prior phases' Settings screen passes, so it is code-reviewed rather than independently re-exercised — flagged for a live pass before Phase 2 (onboarding redesign) ships, since Phase 2 is what will make this the *only* way a fresh Free-plan install reaches the tab shell. **Phases 2-6 (onboarding redesign, ripple cleanup of `isOnlineModeProvider` call sites, Free→Premium shop-identity migration, universal offline-verifiable license fallback, and a client-side Realtime connection-pool safeguard) remain, tracked in the same plan file.** |

| 2026-08-11 | 111 | **Phase C's remaining token retrofit + the pastel status-pill pattern, implemented for the first time.** The color-direction research that started this whole design pass (#106/#107) explicitly called out adopting pastel, not solid, status pills — mint/peach/lavender rather than saturated green/orange/red — and `AppColors` grew a soft-fill tier (`successSurface`/`warningSurface`/`dangerSurface`) for exactly this back in #108, but nothing had actually used it until now. **New shared `StatusPill`/`StatusTone` component** (`core/widgets/app_widgets.dart`): four tones — `positive`/`attention`/`critical`/`neutral` — each a soft-fill background paired with a solid-color label, the `neutral` tone backed by a new `AppColors.neutralSurface` token (the tier didn't have a true neutral before). Adopted in three places that previously hardcoded raw `Color`s: `order_labels.dart`'s `orderStatusColor` (returned `#10B981`/`#9CA3AF`/`#64748B` — a Tailwind emerald that sat on top of the brand green, and two greys with no relationship to the surface ramp) is now `orderStatusTone`, returning a `StatusTone` instead of a `Color` so the two places that render an order's status (list card, detail sheet) can't drift apart. Two judgment calls documented inline: `new` maps to `attention`, not neutral — an order sitting in `new` is the shop's to-do list, waiting on someone to hand it to a carrier, not a passive state; `cancelled` maps to `neutral`, not `critical` — a cancelled order is finished-with, not an error to chase, and a list of red rows for the normal end-state of returns trains the eye to ignore red. `invoice_view.dart`'s paid/partial/unpaid pill and four raw-color spots in `order_detail_sheet.dart` (a check-circle icon, a delete action, a paid/unpaid conditional, a second check icon) now resolve the same way — the delete action correctly stayed a plain danger color, not a status pill, since deleting isn't a state. **Full token retrofit** of `invoice_detail_screen.dart`, `sales_report_screen.dart` (gained a small `_ButtonSpinner` helper for its export/generate buttons' loading state), and `customers_screen.dart` — spacing, type scale, `MoneyText` for amounts, `EmptyStateView` for empty lists. **A third instance of this session's recurring crash, fixed:** `customers_screen.dart`'s `_edit` created *three* `TextEditingController`s (name/phone/address), `await`ed `showDialog`, then disposed all three — the same dispose-after-`await` bug already fixed in `checkout_sheet.dart` (#108) and `categories_screen.dart` (#110), now moved into a `_CustomerEditorDialog` `StatefulWidget` whose lifetime is the dialog's own widget tree. Phone list and tablet grid are now one `_ProductTile` widget instead of two divergent copies of the same row. **Per-row actions restructured.** A thumbnail + a two-line Myanmar name + a stock pill + two 48dp icon buttons does not fit a 402pt phone; the two unlabeled glyphs (print label / update stock, distinguished only by a `tooltip:` that a touch user never sees) moved into a labelled overflow menu, and the *stock pill itself* became the one-tap route to the stock-adjust dialog, so the frequent action costs no extra tap and the rare one gained a translated label. **`_StockBadge` re-toned to three tiers** — out of stock (`dangerSurface`, matching Sell's `qty <= 0` treatment), low (`warningSurface`), healthy (`surfaceContainerHigh`) — with tabular figures and a 48pt minimum width so counts share one right edge. The old healthy fill was `secondaryContainer`, which in the current palette is `#DCE7E1`, byte-for-byte the same sage as `identityFills[0]`: on roughly a quarter of rows the pill and the product plate beside it merged into one shape. The low-stock banner moved off a full-bleed `errorContainer` block onto the `AppColors` soft-fill warning language Sell's two licence banners already use. **New shared `CategoryFilterBar` + `CategoryFilterOption` (`core/widgets/app_widgets.dart`)**, extracted from the version #108 built for Sell and now used by both tabs; Inventory's own copy was still the pre-research pattern (bare `ChoiceChip`s, no counts, a fixed `SizedBox(height: 48)` around translated labels). New `inventoryCategoryCountsProvider` mirrors `sellCategoryCountsProvider`, sharing a `_matchesInventoryQuery` predicate with `filteredProductsProvider` so a chip's count can never disagree with the list. **Tablet grid geometry fixed (found on a real iPad, not reasoned about).** `maxCrossAxisExtent` is a ceiling, not a target — the delegate takes `ceil(width / extent)` columns and divides evenly, so the old 300 produced three ~235pt cards on an 820pt iPad, leaving ~70pt for the name once the fixed-width thumbnail, pill and menu had taken theirs; every Myanmar name shattered into two clipped fragments and "300 ကျပ်" wrapped onto two lines. Now 440 (two ~360pt cards here, never dividing below ~300pt on anything wider) with a `mainAxisExtent` computed from the *scaled* type scale rather than a `childAspectRatio` that ties row height to however many columns happened to fit. The phone list also gained bottom padding so the extended FAB stops covering the last row's menu. **`product_edit_screen.dart`**: the flat 12-field column (with two explanatory paragraphs floating between fields, anchored to nothing) is now four `Card` groups — details / pricing / stock / identification — each hint inside the card it explains; **Save is docked** in a shadowed footer bar like the checkout sheet's confirm bar, instead of sitting at the far end of a long scroll; the photo preview is now itself the tap target (it was a dead 72pt square) and uses `radiusMd` rather than a hardcoded 8. It deliberately keeps its own preview instead of `ProductThumb`: that widget's contract is that a missing photo is a normal designed state, which is right in a list and wrong in the one control whose job is to tell you whether a photo exists. **Functional bug fixed there:** the barcode field inherited the money fields' 9-digit `LengthLimitingTextInputFormatter`, so a hand-typed EAN-13 was silently truncated (scanned values bypass formatters and were unaffected); `_field` now takes a `maxLength` and barcode passes 20. **`stock_history_screen.dart`**: the app-bar subtitle lived in a hard `Size.fromHeight(20)` around a `bodySmall` line that is 20.2pt *before* its 8pt padding and before text scaling, so the product name was clipped on every visit — height is now derived from the scaled line box with room for the second line long Myanmar names need. **`stock_movements_screen.dart`**: subtitles go to two lines (the Myanmar type label plus a 16-character timestamp fills a phone line on its own, so the shopkeeper's note was ellipsized away every time), the empty state is filter-aware (new `stockHistoryEmptyFiltered` / `stockHistoryShowAll` keys — the filters start narrowed by design, so a bare "no stock movements" was routinely reporting an empty ledger that was merely hidden, with no way out), and `opening` joined `_allMovementTypes`: it was missing entirely, so opening-balance rows could only be seen by de-selecting *every* chip, which made ticking all of them a lie and left the new "show all" action unable to do what it says. Both screens' signed deltas now render through `MoneyText` (tabular, right-aligned). **`barcode_scanner_screen.dart`**: the torch button reflects real `TorchState` (it was a fixed `flash_on` glyph that changed nothing when tapped, and is correctly disabled where no torch exists), the viewfinder is sized off the device instead of a fixed 250x160 box, the hint sits inside the safe area on a rounded scrim, and `AppTheme.radius` (the deprecated alias) became `radiusMd`. The white-on-scrim colours are kept and documented as deliberate: the surface behind them is a live camera image, not a theme surface. **`stock_adjust_dialog.dart`** (in scope by adjacency — the new stock pill opens it): Material's segment padding is horizontal-only, so the control stayed pinned at its 40dp minimum height and sliced the second line off "ပစ္စည်းအသစ်ထည့်"; vertical padding lets it grow to whatever the translation needs. Spacing moved onto tokens. **New theme token `AppTheme.dangerFilledButtonStyle`** — the confirm button in a delete dialog was the same brand-green affirmative as "Save", so next to a neutral Cancel the destructive choice read as the safe one; adopted by both Inventory delete confirms, and waiting for the ~10 other delete dialogs in later phases. Categories' rows also lost their edit pencil (the row itself renames, as elsewhere in the app), which stops delete sitting one 8pt gap from the common action. Three new i18n keys total (`commonMore`, `stockHistoryEmptyFiltered`, `stockHistoryShowAll`) in both ARBs + `flutter gen-l10n`. `flutter analyze` clean; **370 tests pass**, no test changes needed (grepped `test/` first — nothing asserts these screens' structure). Ripple check per CLAUDE.md: grepped every reader of `filteredProductsProvider` (semantics unchanged — the predicate was extracted, not altered), `inventoryCategoryProvider`, `inventorySearchProvider` and the new widgets; Sell was re-verified live after the category-bar extraction. **Verified live** on iPhone 17 Pro in `my` × light + dark and `en` × light (Inventory list, low-stock banner, overflow menu, pill-to-stock-dialog, categories add/rename/delete, product editor, both stock ledgers, scanner chrome) and on iPad A16 (`my`, light) for the grid. Noted but not fixed, for later phases: `purchase_order_editor_screen.dart:105` reads `filteredProductsProvider`, so the purchase-order product picker silently inherits whatever search and category the Inventory tab was last left on; and the same dispose-after-`await` controller pattern is still live in `settings_screen.dart:642`, `customers_screen.dart:126`, `suppliers_screen.dart:137` and `purchase_order_editor_screen.dart`. |
| 2026-08-11 | 109 | **Information architecture: bottom nav 6 tabs ➜ 5 — Orders and Invoices merged into one destination with two sub-tabs.** Trigger was a mobile-nav best-practice reference the user shared; its other two points (no hamburger hiding actions, icon+label always shown) were checked and already satisfied — there is no hamburger anywhere in the codebase and the nav already uses `NavigationDestinationLabelBehavior.alwaysShow` — so nav-item count was the only actionable gap. **Before:** six top-level destinations (Sell, Inventory, Orders, Invoices, Analytics, Settings), two of which — `dashboard_customize_outlined` "Orders" and `receipt_long` "Invoices" — are both "a record of a transaction" and sat adjacent. **After:** five (Sell, Inventory, **Orders**, Analytics, Settings), where Orders is a hub with sub-tabs Orders / Invoices. Analytics stays top-level and `ownerOnly` (Staff mode therefore shows 4). **These are NOT duplicates and were not collapsed:** `OrdersScreen` is the pre-sale Social Orders list (Facebook/Viber, before an order becomes a sale), `InvoicesScreen` is the ledger of completed sales — same data sources, same behaviour, same filters, same tablet master–detail; only their *navigation* merged. New `lib/features/orders/orders_invoices_hub_screen.dart` owns a single `Scaffold`; both screens gained an `embedded` constructor flag (default `false`, so standalone use is byte-compatible) that returns body-only. The hub's **chrome follows the selected sub-tab** — title `ordersTitle`/`navInvoices`, Invoices' sales-report `AppBar` action, Orders' "new order" FAB — driven by a `TabController` listener that caches the index and only calls `setState` when it actually changes, because `TabController` notifies on every frame of a swipe (`offset`) and an unguarded rebuild would re-lay-out the whole Scaffold per frame on a low-end panel. Merged destination icon/label: `Icons.receipt_long` + existing `navOrders` key (no new copy; also removes the nav's only outlined icon among otherwise-filled ones). `router.dart`: the two `StatefulShellBranch`es became **one branch with two `GoRoute`s** — `/orders` (first, so it is the branch's initial location) and `/invoices` — each building the hub with a different initial sub-tab, which keeps both URLs alive and Analytics' three `context.go('/invoices')` deep links landing on the Invoices sub-tab specifically. Sub-tab labels reuse the existing `navOrders`/`navInvoices` keys, so **no new ARB strings and no `gen-l10n` run**. Tab height is computed from the label's own line box (`max(48, scaledFontSize × height + space4)`) rather than Material's fixed 46dp, keeping the target ≥48dp and giving Myanmar's stacked diacritics room at any text scale. Ripple check per CLAUDE.md: grepped every `context.go`/`goBranch`/`branchIndex`/`appRouter` reader — the only other route consumer is `context.go('/inventory')` (branch 1, unaffected); `selectedIndex` and the `ownerOnly` filter are index-agnostic (they map filtered position ➜ `branchIndex`) and still bounce to Sell if the current branch becomes hidden. `flutter analyze` clean; **370 tests pass**. Three existing tests asserted the old structure and were updated with the reasoning in comments — `app_smoke_test.dart` (6➜5) and `role_based_tabs_test.dart` (owner 6➜5, staff 5➜4); new `test/orders_invoices_hub_test.dart` (3 cases) pins the chrome swap in both directions and the `/invoices` deep link. Verified live on iPhone 17 Pro in en + my × light + dark (5-tab bar, both sub-tabs, chrome swapping, and the real Analytics KPI-card deep link resolving to Invoices even when the live hub had been left on Orders) and on iPad A16 (`NavigationRail` with 5 items, both sub-tabs' master–detail split panes intact under the hub's app bar). One accepted cosmetic tradeoff: on the Invoices sub-tab the centered title duplicates the tab label; the alternative (a fixed title) moves the same duplication onto Orders, the default and more-visited tab. |
| 2026-08-11 | 108 | **Sell/Checkout structural pass — photo-forward product cards (the reference pattern entries #106/#107 researched but never implemented).** #106 and #107 did real token and palette work on these two screens but left their *structure* untouched, so the single thing every one of the seven approved references (Bitepoint, Cirice, QuickBill, the pastel Restaurant POS, Rabbit, Loyverse) had in common was still missing: products were text only. `sell_screen.dart`'s `_ProductCard` rendered `Expanded(child: Text(name))` + a price — a large blank flexible region shaped exactly like a photo slot nobody had filled — and `itemBuilder` never even passed `imageUrl`, although `Product.imageUrl` exists and `product_edit_screen.dart` has had a working photo-upload flow all along. `checkout_sheet.dart`'s cart lines had no thumbnail either. **New shared component `ProductThumb` (`core/widgets/app_widgets.dart`)** is now the single place a product's mark is drawn — Sell grid, checkout cart lines, and the tablet cart panel all use it, instead of the two hand-rolled `Image.network` call sites that previously existed (`product_edit_screen.dart:282`, `storefront_page.dart:353`) and none on the busiest screen in the app. **The no-photo case is treated as the normal case, not an error case** (a shop keying in 200 items by hand photographs very few): missing URL, failed decode, and *no network at all* all land on the same designed **initials plate** — never a broken-image glyph, never an empty box, and never a spinner that reflows the tile. New theme tokens `AppColors.identityFills`/`identityOnFills` + `identityTone(seed)` back it: four muted tonal plates (sage / teal / slate-blue / lilac), all ≥8:1 with their foreground, deliberately confined to the **cool** half of the wheel because warm hues are spoken for by `warning`/`danger`/`success`, and deliberately **excluding `primaryContainer`** because that fill means "selected/primary" elsewhere and a grid full of it would compete with the one CTA. The plate is hashed from the product name with a hand-rolled hash (not `String.hashCode`, which isn't guaranteed stable across runs), so a product keeps one colour everywhere it appears. Initials are cut on **grapheme clusters**, and Latin gets two letters while Myanmar gets one: applying the two-letter rule to Myanmar produced strings like "အုဘီ" that read as a misspelled word, whereas one cluster ("ကို", "ရေ", "ရွှေ") is a whole syllable and reads as a proper initial; words starting with punctuation are skipped so "ရေသန့် (၁ လီတာ)" doesn't take "(" as its second letter (5 new unit tests in `test/product_thumb_initials_test.dart`). **`_ProductCard` rebuilt**: full-bleed photo band on top, then a name + price block; the name block is pinned to exactly two lines' height *scaled by the current text scaler*, because with a variable-height text block the photo band's height varied per tile and names in a row started at three different heights. Grid geometry retuned for the new content — `childAspectRatio` 1.1 ➜ 0.68 and `maxCrossAxisExtent` 180/210 ➜ 132/168; the delegate counts cross-axis spacing into the extent, so the old 180 was actually producing **two** 183pt tiles on a 402pt phone, and 132 restores three. Out-of-stock now dims the photo, mutes name and price, and lays a soft-fill `dangerSurface` badge over the image (new short i18n key `inventoryOutOfStockBadge` — "Sold out"/"ကုန်ပြီ" — because the full `inventoryOutOfStock` Myanmar string wraps to two lines and covered half the photo, and truncating the label that explains why a tile won't sell wasn't an option). Tapping an out-of-stock tile now calls through to the caller's snackbar instead of being swallowed, which used to leave a tile that ripples under your finger and then does nothing. **`_CartLineTile` (checkout) restructured** to the QuickBill pattern: thumbnail + name + unit price on the left, discount action, then a trailing column with a 40dp-square tonal qty stepper above a right-aligned tabular line total, with hairline separators between lines. **Checkout sheet now has a pinned footer** — the body scrolls, and "Confirm sale" sits in a docked bar carrying the total, the same shape as the Sell screen's docked checkout button; previously the CTA was at the far end of one long scroll (worse now that lines are taller) and slid behind the keyboard as soon as the amount-paid field was focused. The sheet is also capped below the status bar; a full cart used to stretch it to its host's full height, putting the title under the notch and the drag handle off-screen. **`_SellCategoryFilterBar` audited and changed**: it was a bare `ChoiceChip` row in a fixed `SizedBox(height: 48)` — a single-line box around translated labels — carrying no information about what was behind each chip. Chips now show item counts (new `sellCategoryCountsProvider`, counted against the current search via a `_matchesSellQuery` helper shared with `sellProductsProvider` so a chip can never promise rows the grid won't show), sit on thumb-sized padding, and the bar sizes to its content with a hairline above the grid. **Crash fixed, found live during this pass:** `_editLineDiscount` created a `TextEditingController`, `await`ed `showDialog`, then disposed it — but `showDialog`'s future resolves when the route is *popped*, not when its exit animation ends, so the controller was torn out from under a still-mounted `TextField` ("A TextEditingController was used after being disposed") and cascaded into framework assertions and a red screen. The controller now lives in a `_LineDiscountDialog` `StatefulWidget`. The identical pattern still exists in `categories_screen.dart:66-89` (where it was actually observed crashing) and `settings_screen.dart:~642` — logged in `docs/DESIGN_PASS.md` for the Inventory/Settings phases rather than fixed here. Two more new i18n keys (`sellDecreaseQty`/`sellIncreaseQty`) give the qty steppers screen-reader labels. `flutter analyze` clean; **367 tests pass** (362 + 5 new). **Verified live on the iOS Simulator** (iPhone 17 Pro) in **en + my × light + dark**: the grid with real photos *and* with the initials plate, the out-of-stock tile, the category bar with counts, the checkout sheet end-to-end including the per-line discount dialog after the fix — device log clean of Flutter errors afterward. Not observed on screen (deterministic, reasoned from code): the 1-second stock-cap snackbar on an out-of-stock tap, and a product photo *failing* to load (the seed catalogue's URLs all resolved). |

Three new tokens — `successSurface`/`warningSurface`/`dangerSurface` — add a **pastel soft-fill tier** (each verified ≥4.5:1 against its solid partner) so status banners and, later, Orders' pastel status pills stop hand-rolling `color.withValues(alpha: 0.12)`, which rendered muddy over a near-black dark surface; Sell's licence-expiring and read-only banners now use it and finally share one banner language. **`BrandHero`** stopped rendering `assets/branding/app_icon_1024.png` — that placeholder is a gold glyph on a cream plate, so it would have dropped a cream-and-gold rectangle into the middle of every green/white entry screen. It now draws a `primaryContainer` plate with a storefront glyph and a `Semantics` label: recolors correctly in both brightnesses, adds no asset weight, and is a one-widget swap when a real logo exists. **`password_strength.dart`** replaced raw `Colors.orange/amber/green` (never contrast-checked, and the amber step sat in the gold band) with `AppColors.warning`/`.success`/`.danger`. **`checkout_sheet.dart`** adopted the approved reference's icon-bearing payment options: new `paymentIcon()` in `payment_labels.dart` maps method codes to glyphs by *kind of money movement* (cash-in-hand / phone wallet / pay-later / COD / other) so shop-created custom accounts still land sensibly; `showCheckmark: false` because Material paints the selected checkmark on top of the avatar and smudged the chosen method's icon. **Bug fixed, found only by finally getting these screens on a device:** `auth_password_field.dart`'s show/hide `IconButton` had a `tooltip:`, and `app.dart` returns `OnboardingFlow`/`ResetPasswordScreen`/`ModeMigrateFlow`/`OnlineDailyGate` from `MaterialApp.router`'s `builder:`, which sits **above** the Router — so those screens have no `Overlay` ancestor and the Tooltip threw "No Overlay widget found", painting a red error box across the online sign-up form. Live Simulator verification of the Sell screen, Checkout sheet, and the `en`-locale entry/auth screens specifically was **not completed** this session — this sandboxed environment has no touch-injection into the iOS Simulator (no computer-use/idb tool, `osascript`/System Events UI scripting blocked by a missing Accessibility permission, no `xcrun simctl` synthetic-touch command), and a `flutter test`-based screenshot-harness substitute was attempted but hung and was abandoned/deleted rather than left half-working. Remaining phases (Inventory, Orders/Invoices, Analytics/Money, Settings/Back-office, Web surfaces) are tracked in `docs/DESIGN_PASS.md`, not this changelog, until they ship. |

| 2026-08-05 | 59 | **Fixed: a permanently-failing outbox row silently blocked branch switching forever, with the sync status still (misleadingly) showing "Up to date."** Found live, on the owner's physical iPhone, testing #47's branch-switch fix: `Branches → switch` refused with "unsynced changes, wait for sync" even after a manual sync completed and showed "Up to date." Root cause: `SyncEngine._push()`'s per-row error isolation (by design — one bad row must never wedge the queue, see the 0012/CLAUDE.md lesson) catches and swallows every push failure, just incrementing `attempts` and moving on — so a row that can *never* succeed (a "poison pill": stale payload, a since-deleted foreign row, an RLS mismatch) retries forever, completely invisibly, while `SyncController` still reports `idle`/"Up to date" because nothing ever throws up to it. `BranchRepository.switchBranch()`'s outbox-must-be-empty guard (existing, correct, protects against wiping unsynced writes) then blocks forever on that one row with advice ("wait for sync") that can never resolve. Added: **(1)** `Outbox.lastError` (nullable text, schema v27) — the swallowed exception's message is now recorded per row instead of discarded. **(2)** `stuckOutboxProvider` (`sync_providers.dart`) — rows with `attempts >= 3` (`kOutboxStuckThreshold`), the same "isolate but don't hide" philosophy as the push loop itself. **(3)** New `SyncIssuesScreen` (Settings → Cloud sync, a warning row appears only when something's actually stuck) listing each stuck row's table/op/attempt count/last error, with an owner-only **Discard** action (`SyncController.discardOutboxRow`) behind an explicit confirm dialog warning it's real, unrecoverable data loss for that one write — the only way to unwedge a genuine poison pill, since retrying can't. `flutter analyze` clean; 318 tests pass (4 new: `lastError` capture in `sync_engine_test.dart`, `stuckOutboxProvider` threshold + `discardOutboxRow` in new `stuck_outbox_test.dart`). en+my (`syncIssuesTitle`/`Subtitle`/`Empty`/`Attempts`/`Discard`/`DiscardConfirmTitle`/`Body`). No Supabase migration — `Outbox` is a local-only Drift table, never synced itself. |

| 2026-08-05 | 47 | **Fixed 3 branch/shop-switch bugs found on request: Shop Profile bled across shops, Active Staff ID survived a switch, and no feedback during post-switch resync.** Asked directly to investigate reported trouble switching between the main shop and a branch. Deep-read the whole switch path (`BranchRepository.switchBranch`, `AppDatabase.wipeSyncedData`, `SettingsRepository`) and found two real data-scoping bugs plus a UX gap, same class as the already-fixed Payment Accounts seeding flag (#45). **(1)** `SettingsRepository`'s Shop Profile (name/address/phone/logo/receipt footer) was stored under fixed, un-suffixed keys — a single device-global value `wipeSyncedData()` never touched and the `switch_branch` server response never repopulates. Switching to a branch kept showing the previous shop's name/address/logo on receipts, reports, and the Shop Profile screen itself, and *editing* it while on the branch silently overwrote the main shop's profile too (same shared key). `shopProfile()`/`saveShopProfile()`/`setShopLogoUrl()` now take a required `shopId` and key on `'<field>.$shopId'` via a new `_shopKey()` helper — with a deliberate migration path: an **empty** shopId (the `shopIdProvider` default before any license/shop is assigned, e.g. mid-onboarding) maps back to the original un-suffixed key, so a pre-existing single-shop install's data — and onboarding's own profile step, which necessarily runs before a shopId exists — both keep resolving correctly with no data migration; a shop that hasn't set its own profile yet still falls back per-field to that legacy/shared default rather than showing blank, but writes always land on its own key so one shop's edits can never again clobber another's. All 9 read/write call sites updated (`shop_profile_screen.dart`, `onboarding_flow.dart`, `license_repository.dart`'s `startFreeTrial` — now takes `shopId` too — `license_screen.dart` ×2, `referral_screen.dart`, `printing_providers.dart`'s `shopProfileProvider`, plus `print_action.dart`/`order_invoice.dart` switched to read through that provider instead of the repository directly). **(2)** `SettingsRepository`'s `staff.active_id` (which staff member is "using" this device, stamped onto every sale) is device-local by design but `wipeSyncedData()` cleared the shop's `staffMembers` roster without also clearing it — after a switch, sales in the new shop kept getting silently attributed to a dangling staff id from the old shop's now-gone roster. `wipeSyncedData()` now also deletes the `staff.active_id` row. **(3)** `BranchesScreen._confirmSwitch` fired `syncControllerProvider.sync()` without awaiting it and immediately popped the loading spinner + showed a "switched" snackbar — a user who tapped straight into Sell/Inventory right after switching saw an apparently-empty shop (correctly wiped by `wipeSyncedData`, but not yet refilled) with nothing indicating a sync was still in progress. Now awaits `sync()` behind a second non-dismissible spinner dialog (new `branchesSwitchSyncing` copy) before showing the success snackbar. `flutter analyze` clean; 297 tests pass (7 new: shop-profile per-shop isolation + legacy-key fallback in a new `settings_repository_test.dart`, `wipeSyncedData` clearing `staff.active_id`, `LicenseRepository.startFreeTrial`'s test updated for its new `shopId` param). en+my (`branchesSwitchSyncing` — the profile-isolation fix needed no new keys). No schema/migration/Edge Function change — all 3 are local-device data-scoping/reactivity fixes. |

| 2026-08-04 | 39 | **Fixed admin renewal silently creating phantom shops instead of extending the real one; removed dead admin Edge Function actions.** Asked directly to check the admin dashboard because renewal "isn't working." Root cause: `fulfill_request` (the Requests tab's "Issue key" action, the actual approve-a-payment path today) decided renewal-vs-brand-new by looking up `licenses` **by `device_id` alone** — safe back when one device meant one shop, but no longer true now that a shop can have multiple devices (`0025_multi_device_licensing.sql`, Online sign-in on a second device, etc.) and `license_requests` never carried a `shop_id` at all. If the device submitting a request didn't happen to match the exact `device_id` stamped on the shop's `licenses` row, the lookup missed and `fulfill_request` fell through to "issue new" — silently minting a `shop-<request-id>` phantom shop nobody would ever use, while the customer's real, already-paying shop was never extended. Matches the reported symptom exactly: customer pays, admin taps "Issue key," nothing changes. Fixed by adding `shop_id` to `license_requests` (migration `0049`, nullable — genuinely new customers still correctly have none) and having the app send its own `CachedLicense.shopId` whenever one exists (`LicenseRequestService.submit()` gained `shopId`; both `_showOfflineKeyDialog`/`_showOnlineSubscribeDialog` in `license_screen.dart` pass it). `fulfill_request` now looks up by `shop_id` first (matches any of the shop's rows — `renew_license` already extends every row sharing a shop_id, per 0025), falling back to the old `device_id` lookup only when a request has no `shop_id` (already-pending old requests, or truly no prior license). Admin's Requests tab (`_RequestsTab`) now shows whether each row will be treated as a renewal (with its `shop_id`) or a new shop, so a mismatch is visible before tapping Issue rather than trusted blind. Also removed 2 confirmed-dead Edge Function actions found via an audit (grepped every `AdminApi` call site against every `case`): `list_payments` and `renew_license` (the admin-facing action, not the SQL RPC it and `extend_by_device`/`fulfill_request` still call) — both leftovers from the old `license_payments`/"Payments tab" approval flow the client side already dropped (per the earlier "Architecture review fixes" changelog entry), with zero remaining callers. Left the `license_payments` **table** itself alone (still a live Drift-synced table, separate concern). Corrected `lib/admin/README.md`'s stale "Payments" description to describe the real "Requests" tab. `flutter analyze` clean; admin web build verified; all 265 POS tests still pass (an Edge Function's runtime lookup logic isn't unit-testable here — reasoned through the 3 cases: new customer, existing shop via shop_id, old pending request via device_id fallback). No new ARB keys. Migration `0049` applied, `admin` Edge Function redeployed, and admin web redeployed to Vercel — all 2026-08-04 (explicit go-ahead given). |

| 2026-08-04 | 26 | **Third-pass full-app audit — 6 more fixes.** Asked to check again after #25. Ran two more audits (verify #25's fixes — 9/10 confirmed correct, 1 real caveat found and fixed below; a fresh sweep focused on cross-cutting concerns the first two passes wouldn't catch by going feature-by-feature). **(1) Backup restore never reset the restored tables' sync cursors** — found by the verify pass, not the original changelog claim. A restore replaces local data from a snapshot that may predate the shop's current cloud state; without a cursor reset, the next pull's `since` filter would sit past any remote row not in the backup file and permanently skip it. `BackupService.importReplaceAll` now calls a new `SettingsRepository.clearSyncCursor()` for every restored table after the transaction commits, so the next sync re-fetches each table's full history from scratch (safe: every mapper's upsert is idempotent). **(2) Manual stock "Adjust" had no floor** — a fat-fingered or intentional large negative adjustment could silently drive `stock_levels.quantity` negative, corrupting inventory/analytics until noticed. `stock_adjust_dialog.dart` now rejects a delta that would go below zero before even showing the confirm dialog; `InventoryRepository.adjustStock` gained the same floor as a defensive backstop (checked: the only two callers are this dialog and PO-receiving, both now safe; a normal sale's stock decrement is a separate code path in `SalesRepository`, unaffected). **(3)** Storefront `submit_order`'s `orders` and `order_items` inserts weren't atomic — an `order_items` failure after the `orders` insert succeeded left a real, empty order visible in the shop's Orders list; added a compensating delete of the orphaned order on that failure path (no RPC/transaction available from this Edge Function's client). **(4)** Sell screen's "Clear cart" fired immediately with no confirmation, unlike every other destructive action in the app — added one. **(5)** `checkout_sheet.dart`'s per-line discount dialog created a `TextEditingController` that was never disposed — fixed, plus 7 other genuine method-local leaks found across `customers_screen.dart`, `suppliers_screen.dart`, `staff_members_screen.dart`, `branches_screen.dart` (×2), `categories_screen.dart`, `settings_screen.dart`, `license_screen.dart`, and `purchase_order_editor_screen.dart` (×2) — all fixed by disposing right after their `showDialog`/`showModalBottomSheet` await (capturing needed `.text` values into locals first where read after dispose). | 2026-08-04 | 25 | **Second-pass full-app audit — 10 more fixes, including two real data-integrity bugs and a security gap.** Asked to check again, top to bottom, after #24 shipped. Ran two more audits (verify #24's fixes landed correctly — 9/10 confirmed correct, 1 caveat noted below; a fresh sweep for anything else). Fixed in severity order: **(1) `stock_levels.quantity` was synced as an absolute value with last-write-wins — exactly the anti-pattern CLAUDE.md forbids for a counter.** Two offline devices selling the same product would have one's decrement silently overwrite the other's on whichever pushed last. Fixed at the sync-mapper level (`sync_mappers.dart`): a pulled `stock_levels` row now only ever updates `reorder_level` and identity/tombstone fields, never `quantity`; each device instead reconciles its own `quantity` purely by applying `stock_movements` deltas as they're pulled (exactly once per movement — a movement already present locally, e.g. one this device created itself, is never re-applied). New regression test (`sync_stock_reconciliation_test.dart`) simulates two devices adjusting the same product offline and confirms both converge on the correct combined quantity. **(2) Sync pull had no pagination, and a second, subtler bug found by that same regression test: Drift's `DateTimeColumn` truncates to whole-second precision, so two changes within the same second collide on `updated_at`, and the pull's strict `gt` cursor filter would then permanently drop whichever one landed exactly on the next pull's cursor boundary** — silent, permanent data loss on ANY synced table, not just stock. Fixed together in `sync_engine.dart`: `fetchChanges` now paginates in pages of 500 (was uncapped, silently truncated by PostgREST's row cap on a long-offline device), and the cursor filter is now inclusive (`gte`) with a new per-table "already-applied-at-this-timestamp" id set (`SettingsRepository.syncCursorTieIds`) so a boundary row is never silently dropped nor infinitely re-applied. New `sync_engine_test.dart` case covers the same-second-tie scenario directly. **(3) `link_branch` (activate Edge Function) had no rate limiting**, unlike the default `activate` action — it accepts an arbitrary key string and reports back validity, the same brute-force surface, but could be probed with no throttling at all. Extracted the existing per-IP rate limiter into `checkAndRecordRateLimit()`, shared by both. **(4) Backup restore (`BackupService.importReplaceAll`) never enqueued restored rows to the outbox** — a restore only ever took effect locally; the next sync pull could silently overwrite it with whatever's still on the server, and other devices on the shop would never see it. Every restored row is now stamped with a fresh `updatedAt` and enqueued for push, same as any other mutation. **(5)** Admin console (web): delivery-carrier delete had no confirmation at all; device-reset and apply-referral-credit had a code/action prompt but no plain-language consequence — all three now have one, matching the mobile app's confirmation style. **(6)** ~24 screens showed raw exception text (`Text('$e')`, e.g. `SocketException: ...`) instead of a bilingual message on error — replaced with a new shared `commonUnexpectedError` key everywhere. **(7)** Three low-severity items: storefront `order_no` was timestamp-derived and could theoretically collide under concurrent submissions — now derived from the order's own unique id instead; the Purchase Order editor's inline line-delete (within an unsaved draft) had no confirm — added one; `start_trial`'s doc comment overstated its anti-farming guarantee (device_id is a client-generated value, not a hardware fingerprint) — corrected to describe the actual, accepted limitation. `flutter analyze` clean; 256 tests pass (4 new: cross-device stock reconciliation, same-second cursor-tie pull, `receivePO` cancelled-PO no-op already covered by #24, backup-restore outbox enqueue). en+my (`commonUnexpectedError`, `poRemoveLineConfirmTitle`). **Migration/deploy status**: no new Drift schema/migration. | 2026-08-26 | 255 | **Error/bug sweep — 2 critical ripple-effect bugs fixed.** Asked for a complete error/bug sweep before the first Play Store submission. **Bug 1: Cash Register ignored cash-paid Supplier Payments.** The Accounts Payable feature (supplier payments table) was not integrated into the Cash Register's `computeExpectedCash()` function — when a supplier was paid in cash (`method == 'cash'`), the physical money left the drawer but the UI didn't account for it, showing a permanently inflated "Expected cash" figure and allowing negative drawers on settlement. Fixed by: (a) adding `supplierPayments` parameter to `computeExpectedCash()`, (b) adding `supplierPaymentsTotal` to `CashSessionReport`, (c) fetching supplier payments in `expectedCash()` and `reportFor()`, (d) adding `_cashSupplierPaymentsWatchProvider` and having `_CashInputSignal` listen to it, (e) updating the report formatter and PDF to display the line. **Bug 2: Three analytics providers missed sale_items invalidation.** `cumulativeNetProfitProvider`, `pnlStatementProvider`, and `taxStatementProvider` all read from `sale_items` (via SQL JOIN or `summary()`), but only watched `salesStreamProvider` — not `saleItemChangesProvider`. During sync pulls where sale_items arrive after their parent sale, COGS showed as 0 and net profit stayed inflated until any other table changed. Fixed by making `saleItemChangesProvider` public and adding it to all three providers' watch lists. **Test changes:** added provider reactivity test for supplier payments affecting `accountBalanceProvider`; new tests for `_supplierPayment` in `computeExpectedCash`; updated `CashSessionReportFormatter` test for the new line. `flutter analyze` clean; **643/643 tests pass**. |

Root cause, the same class of bug already fixed once this session in a device-name dialog: `showDialog` defaults to pushing onto the **root** navigator, while the screen that opens it (inside a go_router `StatefulShellRoute` tab) has its own **nested branch** navigator. `order_detail_sheet.dart`'s `_confirmDelete`/`_blockCustomer`, `settings_screen.dart`'s track-stock info dialog, and `expense_screen.dart`'s `_delete` all built their `AlertDialog` with `builder: (_) => ...` (discarding the dialog's own context) and then called `Navigator.of(context).pop(...)` using the *outer* screen's context — which resolves to the branch navigator, not the root one the dialog actually lives on. Tapping a button popped the branch navigator (silently dismissing the sheet/screen underneath) while the `AlertDialog` itself, still on the root navigator, stayed stuck on screen looking unresponsive. Grepped every `showDialog` call site in the codebase (18 files) to check for the same pattern — confirmed these were the only 4 affected; every other dialog already correctly captures its own builder context (`ctx`/`dialogContext`). Fixed by using the dialog's own builder context to pop in all 4 spots. `flutter analyze` clean; 194 tests pass (unchanged — a navigator-identity bug isn't exercisable from a widget test without a real nested-router tree; verified manually instead, per this project's convention for UI-only fixes). No new ARB keys. | 2026-07-29 | 3 | **Fix: Inventory had no way to delete a product.** Reported directly after the owner ran a raw SQL `DELETE` against the live Supabase `products`/`sales` tables to clear demo data and found the phone never reflected it — investigating surfaced two separate things. (1) **Root cause of the phone not updating**: this app's sync engine (`lib/data/sync/sync_engine.dart`) pulls changes by `updated_at > last_cursor`; deletes are meant to go through `InventoryRepository.deleteProduct()`/`OrdersRepository.deleteOrder()`, which **soft-delete** (`is_deleted = true`, bumps `updated_at`, enqueues to the outbox) so the tombstone itself syncs down as a normal row change. A raw `DELETE` in Postgres removes the row outright — there's nothing left to pull, so a device that already has that row locally has no way to learn it's gone. Not a bug; explained to the owner, with guidance to also clear the local device (reinstall/clear app storage) to match, since a hard-deleted row can never reconcile via incremental sync. (2) **The actual gap that pushed the owner to raw SQL in the first place**: `product_edit_screen.dart` had a Save button but no Delete at all, even though `InventoryRepository.deleteProduct()` (soft-delete, already tested) existed and was reachable from nowhere in the UI — confirmed via grep before assuming. (Orders already had a working delete action in `order_detail_sheet.dart` — see the fix above for why it wasn't actually working.) Added a delete icon to the product editor's `AppBar` (shown only when editing, not creating) that reuses the exact confirm-dialog pattern already used by `categories_screen.dart`'s delete (same `deleteConfirmTitle`/`commonCancel`/`commonDelete` ARB keys, no new strings needed) and calls the existing `deleteProduct()` — which correctly soft-deletes and syncs both directions going forward. `flutter analyze` clean; 194 tests pass (unchanged — this wires up an already-tested repository method to the UI, no new business logic). No schema/migration change; no new ARB keys. | 2026-07-29 | 2 | **Fix: checkout's customer-name keyboard flipping to numeric mid-typing.** Reported directly. Root cause: `_CustomerAutocomplete` (`checkout_sheet.dart`) instantiated `focusNode: FocusNode()` inline inside its `build()` — but this widget rebuilds on *every keystroke* (its own `onChanged` callback triggers a parent `setState`) and on every customer-directory change, so a brand-new `FocusNode` identity was handed to `RawAutocomplete` on every one of those rebuilds. Recreating the FocusNode mid-typing resets the underlying text input connection, which is what caused the soft keyboard to visibly flip to the wrong type for a moment while the OS renegotiated. Fixed by giving `_CustomerAutocomplete` a stable, `_CheckoutSheetState`-owned `FocusNode` (created once, disposed with the other controllers) passed in as a constructor parameter instead of created inline. `flutter analyze` clean; 184 tests pass (unchanged — a focus-identity bug isn't exercisable from a widget test without driving the real IME, so verified manually instead, per this project's convention for UI-only fixes). | 2026-07-28 | 7 | **Fix: onboarding screen blank on its very first frame (found via tablet testing).** Investigating the "never tested on real tablet hardware" gap (no physical iPad available; used Xcode's iPad Air 11" (M4) Simulator) surfaced a real crash: `BoxConstraints forces an infinite width` from the onboarding screen's bottom `FilledButton`, leaving the whole screen blank except "Skip" and one page-dot. Two wrong turns before landing on the real cause: first suspected a tablet-width bug in `onboarding_flow.dart`; ruled out after it reproduced identically on a phone-width iPhone Simulator and survived a device rotation, at which point it was (also wrongly) written off as a machine-local Flutter/Xcode engine quirk unrelated to app code (superseded — see below). That was corrected by also reproducing it on a **freshly created Android Pixel Tablet emulator** (API 34) — a completely different rendering engine (Android/Skia vs. iOS/Metal) hitting the identical symptom rules out "engine bug on this Mac" and confirms a real app-level issue. Traced the actual crash stack (not just the top assertion) to `FilledButton`'s own internal tap-target sizing (`_RenderInputPadding`, Flutter's `button_style_button.dart`), which on this screen's very first build computed an infinite intrinsic width instead of its real content width — a one-time first-frame race, but because this static screen never naturally triggers a further rebuild, the resulting blank frame never self-heals (matches why it's 100% reproducible in every emulator tried but apparently never observed on a real device). Fixed with a single targeted `ConstrainedBox(maxWidth: 220)` wrapping just the `FilledButton` (`onboarding_flow.dart`) — gives its internals a concrete, finite width regardless of what they compute, invisible in the normal case since the button's natural width is nowhere near 220. Verified by rerunning on both the iPad Simulator and the Android tablet emulator (`adb screencap`): full onboarding content now renders on both. `flutter analyze` clean; 171 tests pass (unchanged — the trigger is a real-environment first-frame timing race that a synthetic widget-test harness can't reproduce deterministically; verified manually instead, consistent with this codebase's existing precedent for UI-only fixes). en+my unaffected (no new strings). See the corrected §13 note (supersedes the same-day "machine-wide, not tablet-specific, no code change" entry, which was itself wrong once the Android repro came in). |

| 2026-07-27 | 9 | **Storefront hardening: 4 gaps closed from the 2026-07-26 abuse audit.** Requested directly, in priority order. (1) **Payment-proof upload limit** — the private `payment-proofs` bucket had no server-side size/type enforcement (client-side `compressImage()`/`FileType.image` are bypassable via a direct Storage API call); bucket now enforces 5 MiB + image-only MIME types (`0032`). (2) **Honeypot + per-IP throttle** — a hidden `hp` field real customers never see (a scripted form-filler that blindly fills every input trips it; server pretends success without writing anything, so the bot gets no signal) plus a hard cap of 5 `submit_order` calls per (shop, IP) per 10 minutes, tracked in a new local-only `storefront_order_attempts` table (`0033`, RLS-enabled with zero policies — only the service role touches it). (3) **Stock warning, not a hard block** — storefront stock can lag the device's synced reality, so an order requesting more than the recorded stock is still accepted, just flagged on new `order_items.low_stock_at_order` (Drift v14→v15) so the owner notices a warning icon on the order-detail line before packing it, instead of promising something they don't have. (4) **Owner block-list** — new `storefront_blocklist` table (`0035`, online-only like `storefronts` itself, not a synced Drift entity), checked by phone before an order is created (403 `blocked`); "Block this customer" action added next to the phone number on the order detail sheet, with a new Settings → My web storefront → **Blocked customers** screen to review/unblock. `flutter analyze` clean; 168 tests pass (no new unit tests — `storefront_repository.dart`/edge-function logic follow the codebase's existing precedent of not being unit-tested, same as the rest of the online-only Supabase-client wrappers). en+my. `storefront` function redeployed; storefront web rebuilt and redeployed to Vercel; migrations `0032`-`0035` applied live. |

Inventory list gained a per-product print icon opening a copies-stepper dialog that prefers the dedicated label printer and falls back to the receipt-printer strip; barcode value falls back `product.barcode → sku → id` so every product is printable even without a real barcode entered. 117 tests pass (8 new: TSPL byte-stream + bitmap-packing tests, barcode-fallback tests). `flutter analyze` clean project-wide; iOS build verified. | 2026-07-23 | 3+9 | **Invoice refunds + barcode + scan-to-search.** Reported directly: customer name/phone weren't showing on the invoice detail screen despite being captured at sale time (`invoice_detail_screen.dart` simply never rendered `s.customerName`/`s.customerPhone` — now shown via the existing `receiptCustomer`/`receiptPhone` labels; the Invoices list subtitle also now shows name+phone for every sale, not just credit ones). **Refunds**: a refund is a normal append-only `Sales` row (never mutates the original — the append-only-ledger invariant holds) with negated subtotal/discount/total/paid and a new `refund_of_sale_id` FK back to the original (Drift v10 + `0024_sale_refunds.sql`, RLS unchanged — same table). `SalesRepository.refundSale()` inserts the reversal sale + reversed sale items + a `'return'` stock movement (restores stock) + a negative payment row (only if something was actually paid) + a closing `CreditPayment` (only if the original had an outstanding balance) — all in one transaction, guarded against double-refund (`StateError`). Because a refund's negated amounts fail the credit book's `paid < total` filter by construction, it can't naturally net out a customer's credit balance the way it nets out revenue/cost — hence the explicit closing `CreditPayment` insert, which lets the existing FIFO repayment allocation handle it like any other repayment. `analytics_calculator.dart`'s `SaleRow` gained `isRefund` so refund rows still net into revenue/cost/discount (automatic via negation) but are excluded from `salesCount`. UI: a Refund button + confirmation dialog on the invoice detail screen, a "Refunded" badge (list + detail), and a full-project audit of `CreditRepository`/`analytics_repository.dart` confirmed no other call site needed changes. **Barcode**: every invoice now renders an in-app Code128 barcode of its invoice/refund number (new `barcode_widget` dependency) and the thermal-printer pipeline emits the same barcode natively via `esc_pos_utils_plus`'s `Generator.barcode()` (Code128 chosen over Code39/EAN/ITF — it's the only one of the four that cleanly encodes the hyphenated alphanumeric invoice numbers). **Search**: the Invoices screen gained a search bar (invoice #, customer name, phone) plus a scan icon that reuses the existing `BarcodeScannerScreen` to fill the search query from a camera scan. 109 tests pass (6 new: 5 refund-repository tests + 1 analytics refund-netting test). `flutter analyze` clean project-wide. Mobile-only change; migration `0024` not yet applied to the live database. |
